<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{t:main}
    Let $0 < \beta < 1$. Given any fixed $\delta > 0$, for all $n \geqslant n_0(\beta,\delta)$, with probability at least $1-\delta$ over $A\sim\mathsf{GOE}(n)$, \prettyref{alg:main} gives a sample from a distribution that is $o_n(1)$ close in TVD error to $\mu_{\beta A}$ in polynomial time.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def:f-tap}
    Denote the TAP free energy for the Hamiltonian $\left\langlem,Am\right\rangle$ at inverse temperature $\beta < 1$ as
    \begin{align}
    \label{e:FTAP}
    \mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(m,y) &:= -\frac{\beta}{2}\langlem,A m\rangle - \langley,m\rangle - \sum_{i=1}^{n} h(m_i) - \frac{1}{4} n\beta^2 \left( {1-\frac{1}{n} \left\lVert{m}\right\rVert_2^2} \right)^2,
    \end{align}
where $h(x) := -\frac{1+x}{2}\log \left( {\frac{1+x}{2}} \right) - \frac{1-x}{2}\log \left( {\frac{1-x}{2}} \right)$ and $\widehat{Q}(m) := \nabla_m^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(m)^{-1}$ where $m \in (-1,1)^n$.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{def:good-points}
    Fix $A \in \mathbb R^{n\times n}$ and $D(m) := \operatorname{\mathsf{diag}}\left(\frac{1}{1-m^2}\right)$ for any $m \in (-1,1)^n$. Let
        \[ \mathcal S_{\beta,A}(c) := \left\{m \in (-1,1)^n \mid c\,D(m) \prec \nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(m)\right\}\,. \]
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:goe-norm-tail}
Let $A \sim \mathsf{GOE}(n)$. Then, 
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:goe-norm-tail}
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\{\ensuremath{\left\lVert A \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}>3\}\leqslant2e^{-n/4}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
This is given by the Gaussian eigenvalue tail bound of \cite[Theorem II.11]{davidson2001local} applied to $A$ and $-A$.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{l:W-gw}
Consider the setup of \eqref{e:sdes} where $X_t$ and $\widehat X_t$ are diffusion processes, that is, $f_t = f_t(X_t)$, $\Sigma_t = \Sigma_t(X_t)$, $\widehat f_t = \widehat f_t(X_t)$, $\widehat \Sigma_t = \widehat \Sigma(\widehat X_t)$.
Suppose also that for $t\in [0,T]$,
\begin{enumerate}
    \item 
    $\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {
    \mathds{1}_{\tau(\widehat X_t)>t}
    \left\Vert {f_t(\widehat X_t)-\widehat f_t(\widehat X_t)}\right\Vert^2} \right]\leqslant \varepsilon_f(t)^2$ and 
    $\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {
    \mathds{1}_{\tau(X_t)>t}
    \left\Vert {\Sigma_t(X_t)-\widehat \Sigma_t(X_t)}\right\Vert_F^2} \right]\leqslant \varepsilon_\Sigma(t)^2$, where $X_t, \widehat X_t$ evolve according to the SDE \eqref{e:sdes}.
    \item 
    $f_t$ is $L_f$-segmentwise Lipschitz and 
    $\widehat \Sigma_t$ is $L_\Sigma$-segmentwise Lipschitz on $\mathcal S$ (with respect to the $\left\Vert {\cdot}\right\Vert_2$ and $\left\Vert {\cdot}\right\Vert_F$ norms),  
    for some $L_f, L_\Sigma>0$.
    \item 
    $\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}(X_{[0,T]} \in \mathfrak S_T(r)) \geqslant 1-\delta_1$.
\end{enumerate}
    Then for $t\in [0,T]$, 
    with $X_t$ and $\widehat X_t$ driven by the same Brownian motion in \eqref{e:sdes} (i.e. synchronous coupling), 
we have 
    \[
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\mathds{1}_{\mathfrak S^2_t(r)}[X_{[0,t]}, \widehat X_{[0,t]}]\left\Vert {X_t -\widehat X_t}\right\Vert^2} \right] \leqslant 
\int_0^t 
\left( {\frac{\sqrt 2}{L_f} \varepsilon_f(s)^2 + 2\varepsilon_\Sigma(s)^2} \right)
e^{(2\sqrt 2 L_f + 2L_\Sigma^2)(t-s)}ds.
    \]
    Moreover, if $r_2\leqslant r$ and $t\leqslant \frac{1}{2L_1}\log r_2$,
    \[
\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}[(X_{[0,t]}, \widehat X_{[0,t]}) \nin \mathfrak S^2(r,r_2)] \leqslant 
\delta_1 + 
\frac{32 tL_\Sigma^2 + \int_0^t 32 \varepsilon_\Sigma(s)^2 + 8 \varepsilon_f(s)\,ds}{\log r_2} + \frac{1}{r_2}.
    \]
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- prf-1: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal F_t$ be the filtration for the Brownian motion.
    Let $\Delta_t:= X_t-\widehat X_t$. 
    Note 
    \[
d\Delta_t = (f_t(X_t) - \widehat f_t(\widehat X_t))dt
+ (\Sigma_t(X_t) - \widehat \Sigma_t(\widehat X_t))dB_t.
    \]
    By Itô's formula, for $t\geqslant u$, 
    \allowdisplaybreaks
    \begin{align*}
d\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert^2 &= 
2\left\langle\Delta_t, (f_t(X_t) - \widehat f_t(\widehat X_t))dt
+ (\Sigma_t(X_t) - \widehat \Sigma_t(\widehat X_t))dB_t\right\rangle
+ \left\Vert {\Sigma_t(X_t) - \widehat \Sigma_t(\widehat X_t)}\right\Vert_F^2dt\\
\implies
\frac{d}{d t}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert^2 \mid \mathcal F_u} \right]
&= 2 \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\left\langle\Delta_t, f_t(X_t) - \widehat f_t(\widehat X_t)\right\rangle \mid \mathcal F_u} \right] + 
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\left\Vert {\Sigma_t(X_t) - \widehat \Sigma_t(\widehat X_t)}\right\Vert_F^2\mid \mathcal F_u} \right]\\
&\leqslant c\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert^2\mid \mathcal F_u} \right] + \frac{1}{c}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ { \left\Vert {f_t(X_t)-\widehat f_t(\widehat X_t)}\right\Vert^2\mid \mathcal F_u} \right] + \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ { \left\Vert {\Sigma_t(X_t) - \widehat \Sigma_t(\widehat X_t)}\right\Vert_F^2\mid \mathcal F_u} \right]
    \end{align*}
    for any $c>0$.
Now for $x\in \mathfrak S(r)$ and $\widehat x\in B(x,r)$, by the definition of $\mathfrak S(r)$ and segmentwise Lipschitzness on $\mathcal S$, 
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \left\Vert {\widehat f_t(\widehat x) - f_t(x)}\right\Vert^2 &\leqslant 2 \left\Vert { f_t(\widehat x) - f_t(x)}\right\Vert^2 + 
    2 \left\Vert {\widehat f_t(\widehat x) - f_t(\widehat x)}\right\Vert^2
    \leqslant 2L_f^2 \left\Vert {\widehat x - x}\right\Vert^2 + 2  
    \left\Vert {\widehat f_t(\widehat x) - f_t(\widehat x)}\right\Vert^2
    \\
    \left\Vert {\widehat \Sigma_t(\widehat x) - \Sigma_t(x)}\right\Vert_F^2
    &\leqslant 2\left\Vert {\widehat \Sigma_t(\widehat x) - \widehat \Sigma_t(x)}\right\Vert_F^2 + 2\left\Vert {\widehat \Sigma_t(x) -  \Sigma_t(x)}\right\Vert_F^2
        \leqslant 
        2L_{\Sigma}^2\left\Vert {\widehat x - x}\right\Vert^2 + 2  
        \left\Vert {\widehat \Sigma_t(x) -  \Sigma_t(x)}\right\Vert_F^2.
    \end{align*}
    Plugging in gives that for $t=u$, on the event $\mathfrak S^2(r)$, 
    \allowdisplaybreaks
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{d}{d t}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert^2\mid \mathcal F_u} \right]
        &\leqslant 
        \left( {c+\frac{2L_f^2}{c} + 2L_\Sigma^2} \right) \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert^2\mid \mathcal F_u} \right] + \frac{2}{c} \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\left\Vert {\widehat f_t(\widehat X_t) - f_t(\widehat X_t)}\right\Vert^2\mid \mathcal F_u} \right] + 2 \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\left\Vert {\widehat \Sigma_t(X_t) -  \Sigma_t(X_t)}\right\Vert_F^2\mid \mathcal F_u} \right]\\
        &= 
        \left( {2\sqrt 2 L_f + 2L_\Sigma^2} \right) \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert^2\mid \mathcal F_u} \right] + \frac{\sqrt 2}{L_f} \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\left\Vert {\widehat f_t(\widehat X_t) - f_t(\widehat X_t)}\right\Vert^2\mid \mathcal F_u} \right] + 2\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\left\Vert {\widehat \Sigma_t(X_t) -  \Sigma_t(X_t)}\right\Vert_F^2\mid \mathcal F_u} \right]
    \end{align*}
     by taking $c=\sqrt 2 L_f$.
Now take $L = 2\sqrt 2 L_f + 2L_\Sigma^2$ and  
$E_t(x,\widehat x) = \frac{\sqrt2}{L_f}\left\Vert {\widehat f_t(\widehat x) - f_t(\widehat x)}\right\Vert^2 + 2\left\Vert {\widehat \Sigma_t(x) -  \Sigma_t(x)}\right\Vert_F^2$, and $\varepsilon(t) = \frac{\sqrt 2}{L_f} \varepsilon_f(t)^2 + 2\varepsilon_\Sigma(t)^2$.
This shows that for $t=u$, on the event $\mathfrak S^2(r)$,
\[
        \frac{d}{d t}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} [M_t \mid \mathcal F_u]\leqslant 0
        \text{ where }
        M_t = e^{-Lt} \left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert^2 - \int_0^t  
        E_t(X_t, \widehat X_t) e^{-Ls}\,ds 
\]
Define the stopping time 
\[
\tau_2 = \inf\left\{{t\in [0,T]}:{(X_{[0,t]}, \widehat X_{[0,t]})\nin \mathfrak S^2_t(r)}\right\}.
\]
On the event that $\tau_2 \leqslant u$, we have $\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\left\Vert {\Delta_{t\wedge \tau_2}}\right\Vert^2 \mid \mathcal F_u]=\left\Vert {\Delta_{\tau_2}}\right\Vert^2$, so the above chain of inequalities also holds. Hence 
\[
        \frac{d}{d t}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {M_{t\wedge \tau_2}} \right] \leqslant 0.
\]
Hence
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[ \mathds{1}_{\mathfrak S_t^2(r)}[X_{[0,t]}, \widehat X_{[0,t]}] \left\Vert {X_t-\widehat X_t}\right\Vert^2] \leqslant \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\left\Vert {\Delta_{t\wedge \tau_2}}\right\Vert^2} \right] &\leqslant 
\int_0^t 
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{\mathfrak S^2(r)}[X_{[0,t]}, \widehat X_{[0,t]}] E_t(X_t,\widehat X_t)} \right]
e^{L(t-s)}ds\\
&\leqslant \int_0^t 
\varepsilon(s)
e^{L(t-s)}ds.
\end{align*}
The second part follows from the next lemma, \prettyref{l:safe-pair} with $L_b = L_f$, $\varepsilon_b(t) = \varepsilon_f(t)$, $L_C=L_\Sigma$, $\varepsilon_C(t) = \varepsilon_\Sigma(t)$.
<!-- end prf-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{l:safe-pair}
    Consider the setup of \eqref{e:sdes}, and suppose that
    $\Delta_t = X_t - \widehat X_t$ satisfies
    \[
d\Delta_t = b_t \,dt + C_t\,dB_t.
    \]
Define the stopping time $\tau_2 = \inf\left\{{t\geqslant 0}:{(X_t,\widehat X_t)\nin \mathcal S^2(r)}\right\}$.
Suppose that we can write $b_t = b_t^{\mathrm{Lip}} + b_t^{\mathrm{err}}$ and 
$C_t = C_t^{\mathrm{Lip}} + C_t^{\mathrm{err}}$ such that when $(X_t, \widehat X_t)\in \mathcal S^2(r)$,
\begin{align*}
    \left\Vert {b_t^{\mathrm{Lip}}}\right\Vert &\leqslant L_b \left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert,&
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\mathds{1}_{t<\tau_2}\left\Vert {b_t^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert^2} \right] &\leqslant \varepsilon_b(t)^2\\
    \left\Vert {C_t^{\mathrm{Lip}}}\right\Vert_F &\leqslant L_C \left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert,&
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\mathds{1}_{t<\tau_2}\left\Vert {C_t^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert_F^2} \right] &\leqslant \varepsilon_C(t)^2.
\end{align*}
Suppose that $\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}(X_{[0,t]}\nin\mathfrak S_t(r)) \leqslant \delta_1$.
Then if $r\leqslant r_2$ and $t\leqslant \frac{1}{2L_1}\log r_2$,
    \[
\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}[(X_{[0,t]}, \widehat X_{[0,t]}) \nin \mathfrak S^2(r,r_2)] \leqslant 
\delta_1 + 
\frac{32 tL_C^2 + \int_0^t 32 \varepsilon_C(s)^2 + 8 \varepsilon_b(s)\,ds}{\log r_2} + \frac{1}{r_2}.
    \]
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- prf-2: body_md -->
We will use a (super)martingale concentration inequality. Let $r_t = \sqrt{\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert^2+1}$.
By It\^o's formula,
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    d\log (\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert^2 + 1) 
    & = \frac{2\left\langle\Delta_t, b_t \,dt + C_t\,dB_t\right\rangle}{r_t^2}  
    + \frac{2\left\Vert {C_t}\right\Vert_F^2}{r_t^2}\,dt
    - \frac{\left\Vert {C_t^\top \Delta_t}\right\Vert^2}{r_t^4}dt.
\end{align*}
On the event $\mathfrak S_t^2(r)$, 
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\left\Vert {b_t}\right\Vert}{r_t} &\leqslant 
    \frac{\left\Vert {b_t^{\mathrm{Lip}}}\right\Vert}{r_t} + 
    \frac{\left\Vert {b_t^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert}{r_t}
    \leqslant L_b  + 
    \left\Vert {b_t^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert\\
    \frac{\left\Vert {C_t}\right\Vert_F^2}{r_t^2} 
    &\leqslant \frac{2\left\Vert {C_t^{\mathrm{Lip}}}\right\Vert_F^2}{r_t^2} + \frac{2\left\Vert {C_t^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert_F^2}{r_t^2}
        \leqslant 
        2L_C^2 
         +  
        2\left\Vert {C_t^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert_F^2.
\end{align*}
Hence, for $t<\tau_2$, 
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    d\log (\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert^2 + 1) 
    & \leqslant \left( {2L_b + 4L_C^2 + 2 \left\Vert {b_t^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert + 4\left\Vert {C_t^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert_F^2} \right)dt + \frac{2\left\langle\Delta_t, C_t\,dB_t\right\rangle}{r_t^2}.
\end{align*}
Hence, letting $L_1 = 2L_b + 4L_C^2$, $F_t =2 \left\Vert {b_t^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert + 4\left\Vert {C_t^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert_F^2$, and $N_t = \log (\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert^2 + 1)  - L_1 t - \int_0^t F_s\,ds$, for $t<\tau_2$,
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    dN_t \leqslant \frac{2\left\langle\Delta_t, C_t\,dB_t\right\rangle}{r_t^2}.
\end{align*}
The quadratic variation is bounded as 
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    [N]_{t\wedge \tau_2} &\leqslant \int_0^{t\wedge \tau_2} \frac{4\left\Vert {\Delta_s}\right\Vert^2 \ensuremath{\left\lVert C_s \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}^2}{r_s^4}\,ds\leqslant \int_0^{t\wedge \tau_2} \frac{4\left\Vert {C_s}\right\Vert_F^2}{r_s^2}\,ds
    \leqslant \int_0^{t\wedge \tau_2} 8L_C^2 + 8 \left\Vert {C_s^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert_F^2\,ds\leqslant 8tL_C^2 +  8\int_0^{t\wedge \tau_2} \left\Vert {C_s^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert_F^2\,ds.
\end{align*}
We have the martingale concentration bound
\begin{align*}
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}} \left( {\sup_{s\leqslant t\wedge \tau_2} N_s \geqslant x, \, [N]_{t\wedge \tau_2} \leqslant v} \right)\leqslant e^{-\frac{x^2}{2v}}.
\end{align*}
We now take expectations for $[N]_{t\wedge \tau_2}$ and the random part of $N_{t\wedge \tau_2}$.  
We have
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[N]_{t\wedge \tau_2} &\leqslant 8 tL_C^2 + 8\int_0^t \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{s\leqslant \tau_2}\left\Vert {C_s^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert_F^2} \right]\,ds
    \leqslant 8 tL_C^2 + 8\int_0^{t} \varepsilon_C(s)^2\,ds=:A\\
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \int_0^{t\wedge \tau_2} F_s\,ds &\leqslant \int_0^t 2\varepsilon_b(s) + 4 \varepsilon_C(s)^2 \,ds=:B.
\end{align*}
By Markov's inequality, with probability $1-\delta$,
\begin{align*}
    [N]_{t\wedge \tau_2} &\leqslant \frac{2}{\delta} A,& 
    \int_0^{t\wedge \tau_2} F_s\,ds &\leqslant \frac{2}{\delta} B.  
\end{align*}
Let $\mathcal A$ be the event when both these hold. Then 
\begin{align*}
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}} \left( {\sup_{s\leqslant t\wedge \tau_2} N_s \geqslant x, \, \mathcal A} \right)\leqslant \exp\left( {-\frac{x^2}{4A/\delta}} \right).
\end{align*}
Note that if $\sup_{s\leqslant t\wedge \tau_2} \left\Vert {\Delta_s}\right\Vert^2 \geqslant e^{L_1t+\frac{2}{\delta}B+x}$, under $\mathcal A$, then the above event holds. 
We have, for $e^{L_1s + \frac{2}{\delta}B+x}\leqslant r$ and $x\geqslant 0$, 
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    &\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left( {\{\forall t\in [0,T],\, X_t\in \mathcal S\}^c\cup \left\{ {\sup_{s\leqslant t}\left\Vert {\Delta_s}\right\Vert^2 \geqslant e^{L_1t + \frac{2}{\delta}B+x}} \right\}} \right)\\
    &\leqslant 
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left( {\{\forall t\in [0,T],\, X_t\in \mathcal S\}^c} \right)
    + 
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left( {\mathcal A^c} \right)
    + 
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left( {\{\forall t\in [0,T],\, X_t\in \mathcal S\}\cap \mathcal A\cap \left\{ {\sup_{s\leqslant t\wedge \tau_2}\left\Vert {\Delta_s}\right\Vert^2 \geqslant e^{L_1t + \frac{2}{\delta}B+x}} \right\}} \right)\\
    &\leqslant \delta_1 + \delta + \exp\left( {-\frac{x^2}{4A/\delta}} \right).
\end{align*}
The first inequality is because, if  $\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert^2 \geqslant e^{L_1s + \frac{2}{\delta}B+x}$, then either $t<\tau_2$ and hence $\sup_{s\leqslant t\wedge \tau_2} \left\Vert {\Delta_s}\right\Vert^2\geqslant e^{L_1s + \frac{2}{\delta}B+x}$, or $t\geqslant \tau_2$. In the latter case, 
\[
\sup_{s\leqslant t\wedge \tau_2} \left\Vert {\Delta_s}\right\Vert^2\geqslant
\left\Vert {\Delta_{\tau_2}}\right\Vert^2\geqslant r\geqslant e^{L_1s + \frac{2}{\delta}B+x}
\]
as well. Choosing $x$ such that $e^{L_1s + \frac{2}{\delta}B+x}=r_2^2\leqslant r^2$ gives
\[
\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left[ {(X_{[0,t]}, \widehat X_{[0,t]}) \nin \mathfrak S^2(r,r_2)} \right]
\leqslant 
\delta_1 + \delta + \exp\left( {-\frac{\delta(2\log r_2 - L_1 t - \frac{2B}{\delta})^2}{4A}} \right).
\]
If $L_1t\leqslant \frac{1}{2}\log r_2$, then taking $\delta = \frac{4\max\{A,B\}}{\log r_2}$ gives that this is $\leqslant \delta_1 + \frac{4\max\{A,B\}}{\log r_2} + \frac{1}{r_2}$.
<!-- end prf-2 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{l:m-error}
Suppose that \ref{d:Q-error}, \ref{d:safe-set-stay-inside}, \ref{d:Q-reg}, \ref{d:drift-error}, and \ref{d:Q-Lip} hold to time $T$.
    Let $y_t$ denote the SL process, and $m_t=\left\langle\sigma\right\rangle_{y_t}$ be the true magnetization for tilt $y_t$.  
    Then for each $t\in [0,T]$ there is a constant $C(t)$ such that 
    \[
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{\mathcal G_t}\left\Vert {m_t^y-m_t}\right\Vert^2} \right]\leqslant C(t),
    \]
    where $\mathcal G_t = \{((m,y)_{[0,t]}, (\widehat{m},\widehat{y})_{[0,t]})
\in \mathfrak S_t^2(\rho\sqrt n,c\rho\sqrt n/2)\}$ and $\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal G_t)=1-o_n(1)$. Here, $c=c_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(\beta, T)$ and $\rho = \rho(\beta, T)$ as in \ref{d:safe-set-stay-inside}.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- prf-3: body_md -->
Well-posedness of the SDE's is proven by \prettyref{lem:stay-in-cube}.
Consider the synchronously coupled SDE's
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    (\textup{PHD-TAP})\quad &
    \begin{cases}
        d\widehat{m}_t = \widehat Q(\widehat{m}_t)dB_t\\
        d\widehat{y}_t = f(\widehat{m}_t)dt + dB_t
    \end{cases} \\
    (\textup{Ideal})\quad &
    \begin{cases}
        dm_t= Q(m_t)dB_t = \mathop{{}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Cov}}}(\mu_{y_t}) dB_t\\
        dy_t= m_tdt+dB_t
    \end{cases}
\end{align*}
over $\Omega:= (-1,1)^n\times \mathbb R^n$. 
Then
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A, B_t}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)}[m_t] \left\Vert {Q(m_t) - \widehat{Q}(m_t)}\right\Vert_F^2} \right]
    & \leqslant 
     \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A, B_t}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)} [m_t]\ensuremath{\left\lVert \widehat{Q}(m_t) \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}^2 \left\Vert {Q(m_t) - \widehat{Q}(m_t)}\right\Vert_F^2} \right]\\
     &\leqslant_{\textup{\ref{d:safe-set-stay-inside}}}
     c^{-2} \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A, B_t}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)}  [m_t]\left\Vert {\widehat{Q}(m_t)^{-1}Q(m_t) - I_n}\right\Vert_F^2} \right] \\
     &= c^{-2}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A, B_t}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)}[m_t]\left\lVert{\nabla_m^2\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(m_t)
        Q_t - I_n}\right\rVert^2_F} \right]\\
    &\leqslant_{\textup{\ref{d:Q-error}}} c^{-2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}}(\beta, t)^2\,.
\end{align*}
Therefore, by Markov's inequality, for any $\delta$, with probability $\geqslant 1-\delta$ over $A$, 
\[
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{B_t}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)}[m_t] \left\Vert {Q(m_t) - \widehat{Q}(m_t)}\right\Vert_F^2} \right] = O(1)
\]
where we consider $\beta, T,\delta$ fixed. We also have, with probability $\geqslant 1-o_n(1)$ over $A$, 
\begin{align*}
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{B_t} \left[ {\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)}[\widehat{m}_t] \left\Vert {f(\widehat{m}_t) - \widehat{m}_t}\right\Vert^2} \right]
&\leqslant_{\textup{\ref{d:drift-error}}} \varepsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}^2 = O(1).
\end{align*}
By \ref{d:Q-Lip}, we have that $\widehat{Q}$ is $O(1)$-Lipschitz with respect to $\left\Vert {\cdot}\right\Vert_{\Omega}$, and the identity map is clearly 1-Lipschitz. \ref{d:safe-set-stay-inside} supplies $1-o_n(1)$ probability of $(m_t,y_t)$ staying inside $\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$. 
Define $\mathfrak S_t^2(r_1,r_2)$ using $\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$ as the safe set. 
Then \prettyref{l:W-gw} applies to give 
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{\mathfrak S_t^2(\rho\sqrt n)}[(m,y)_{[0,t]}, (\widehat{m},\widehat{y})_{[0,t]}] 
\left\Vert {(m_t,y_t) - (\widehat{m}_t,\widehat{y}_t)}\right\Vert^2
} \right] &= O(1)\\
\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}[((m,y)_{[0,t]}, (\widehat{m},\widehat{y})_{[0,t]})\nin \mathfrak S_t^2(\rho\sqrt n, \rho\sqrt n/2)] &= o_n(1).
\end{align*}
Consider the map $\widehat{y}$ given by \eqref{e:hy}. 
We aim to apply \prettyref{l:inv-f}, but because the ball is truncated by the cube, we work in dual space, $x=\tanh^{-1}(m)$. First, we claim that for $m\in (-1,1)^n$, $B(\tanh^{-1}(m),r)\subseteq \tanh^{-1}(B(m,r)\cap (-1,1)^n)$. Since $\tanh:\mathbb R^n\to (-1,1)^n$ is a homeomorphism, this is equivalent to $\tanh(B(\tanh^{-1}(m),r))\subseteq B(m,r)\cap (-1,1)^n$. This is clear since $\tanh$ is contractive.

Now consider $\widehat y\circ \tanh: \tanh^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c))\to \mathbb R^n$. Note $D(\widehat y\circ \tanh)(\tanh^{-1}(m))\succeq \widehat{Q}(m)^{-1}D(m)^{-1} \succeq cI_n$. 
When $((m,y)_{[0,t]}, (\widehat{m},\widehat{y})_{[0,t]})\in \mathfrak S_t^2(\rho\sqrt n, \rho\sqrt n/2)$, $B(\tanh^{-1}(\widehat{m}_t), \rho\sqrt n/2)\subseteq \tanh^{-1}(B(\widehat m_t, \rho\sqrt n/2)\cap (-1,1)^n)\subseteq \tanh^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c))$, so by \prettyref{l:inv-f}, 
\[B(\widehat{y}(\widehat{m}_t) = \widehat{y}_t, c\rho\sqrt n/2) \subseteq 
\widehat{y}(B(\widehat m_t, \rho\sqrt n/2)\cap (-1,1)^n)
\subseteq \widehat{y}(\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)).\] 
Now let 
\[\mathcal G_t = \{((m,y)_{[0,t]}, (\widehat{m},\widehat{y})_{[0,t]})
\in \mathfrak S_t^2(\rho\sqrt n,c\rho\sqrt n/2)\}.\] 
Under $\mathcal G_t$, for all $s\in [0,t]$,
$y_s\in B(\widehat{y}_s,c\rho\sqrt n/2)$,   
so  considering the local inverse map $\widehat{m}_{\widehat{y}_s}:B(\widehat{y}_s, c\rho\sqrt n/2)\to B(\widehat{m}_s, \rho\sqrt n/2)$, by continuity of $m^y_s$, we have that $\widehat{m}_{\widehat{y}_s}(y_s)= m^y_s$ and
\[
\left\Vert {m^y_s - \widehat{m}_s}\right\Vert
= \left\Vert {\widehat{m}_{\widehat{y}_s}(y_s) - \widehat{m}_{\widehat{y}_s}(\widehat{y}_s)}\right\Vert
\leqslant c^{-1}\left\Vert {y_s-\widehat{y}_s}\right\Vert.
\]
since $D\widehat{m}_{\widehat{y}_s}(y) = \widehat{Q}(\widehat{m}_{\widehat{y}_s}(y))$ has operator norm at most $c^{-1}$.
Hence under $\mathcal G_t$,
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{\mathcal G_t}\left\Vert {m_t- m_t^y}\right\Vert^2} \right]
    &\lesssim
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal G_t}\left\Vert {m_t-\widehat{m}_t}\right\Vert^2] + \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal G_t}\left\Vert {\widehat{m}_t-m_t^y}\right\Vert^2] \\
    &\lesssim  \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal G_t}\left\Vert {m_t-\widehat{m}_t}\right\Vert^2] + \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal G_t}\left\Vert {\widehat{y}_t-y_t}\right\Vert^2] 
    =O(1). \qedhere
\end{align*}
\begin{lemma}[{Local Hadamard  Theorem, \cite[5.3.11]{ortega2000iterative}}]\label{l:inv-f}
    Let $f:\Omega\to \mathbb R^n$ be a continuously differentiable map where $\Omega \subseteq \mathbb R^n$ is open. Suppose $B(m_0,r)\subseteq \Omega$ and $Df\succeq cI_n$ on $\Omega$, $c>0$. Then $B(f(m_0), cr)\subseteq f(\Omega)$.
    Moreover, $f$ is injective on any convex subset of $\Omega$.
\end{lemma}
<!-- end prf-3 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{l:inv-f}
    Let $f:\Omega\to \mathbb R^n$ be a continuously differentiable map where $\Omega \subseteq \mathbb R^n$ is open. Suppose $B(m_0,r)\subseteq \Omega$ and $Df\succeq cI_n$ on $\Omega$, $c>0$. Then $B(f(m_0), cr)\subseteq f(\Omega)$.
    Moreover, $f$ is injective on any convex subset of $\Omega$.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{c:O1-KL}
Suppose that \ref{d:Q-error}, \ref{d:safe-set-stay-inside}, \ref{d:Q-reg}, \ref{d:drift-error}, and \ref{d:Q-Lip} hold to time $T$. Let $y_t$ and $\widehat{y}_t$ follow (SL) and (ASL-TAP) (with any extension outside of $\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$).  
Then there is a distribution $Q_T$ such that 
\[
\operatorname{TV}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}}(y_T), Q_T) = o_n(1), \quad 
\operatorname{KL}(Q_T\|\operatorname{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{y}_T)) = O(1).
\]
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- prf-4: body_md -->
Let $\tau=\inf\left\{{t\geqslant0}:{((m_t,y_t),(\widehat{m}_t,\widehat{y}_t))\nin \mathcal S_t^2(\rho\sqrt n, c\rho\sqrt n/2)}\right\}$. 
Let $\mathbf{P}_T$ and $\widehat \mathbf{P}_T$ denote the path measures of $(y_{t\wedge \tau})_{t=0}^T$ and $(\widehat{y}_{t\wedge \tau})_{t=0}^T$. 
Since $m^y_t , m_t$ are bounded, Novikov's condition holds: $\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \exp\left( {\frac{1}{2} \int_0^T \left\Vert {m^y_t - m_t}\right\Vert^2dt} \right)<\infty$. 
By the data processing inequality and Girsanov's Theorem,
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \operatorname{KL}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}}(y_{T\wedge \tau})\|\operatorname{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{y}_{T\wedge \tau}))
    &\leqslant 
    \operatorname{KL}(\mathbf{P}_T\|\widehat \mathbf{P}_T)
    \leqslant 
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{P}_T}
    \log \frac{d \mathbf{P}_T}{d \widehat \mathbf{P}_T}\\
    &=
    -\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}  
    \int_0^{T\wedge \tau} \langlem_t - m^y_t, dB_t\rangle + \frac{1}{2} \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}
    \int_0^{T\wedge \tau} \left\Vert {m_t - m^y_t}\right\Vert^2dt\\
    &= \frac{1}{2} 
    \int_0^{T\wedge \tau} \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\Vert {m_t - m^y_t}\right\Vert^2\,dt = O(1),
\end{align*}
    where the last equality follows from \prettyref{l:m-error}. Noting that $\operatorname{TV}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}}(y_{T\wedge \tau}), \operatorname{\mathcal{L}}(y_T)), \operatorname{TV}(\operatorname{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{y}_{T\wedge \tau}), \operatorname{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{y}_{T}))\leqslant \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}[\tau<T] = o_n(1)$ finishes the proof.
<!-- end prf-4 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{l:je-survival}
Let $\tau = \inf\left\{{t\geqslant 0}:{m_t \nin \mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)}\right\}$ and $S_T = \{\tau>T\}$. For a measure $\mathbf{Q}_T$ on path space $m:[0,T]\to \mathbb R^n$, let $\mathbf{Q}_T^s (\cdot) = \mathbf{Q}_T^s(\cdot \cap S_T)$, and let $q_T^s(m) = \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{m_{[0,T]}\sim \mathbf{Q}_T}[\mathds{1}_{S_T} \mid m_T = m]$, i.e., the marginal distribution of $m_T$ under $\mathbf{Q}_T^s$. 
Let $\mathbf{P}_T$ denote the path measure $m_{[0,T]}$ in \eqref{e:SL-TAP} and $\widehat \mathbf{P}_T$ denote the path measure of $\widehat{m}_{[0,T]}$ in the \eqref{e:ASL-TAP}. 
    Then we have that
    \allowdisplaybreaks
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^s}{d \widehat \mathbf{P}_T^s}(m_{[0,T]})
        &= e^{w_T}\cdot 
        \frac{e^{-\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(0,0)}}{e^{-\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(m_T, \widehat{y}(m_T))}} \cdot e^{-\frac{nT}{2}} \cdot \frac{Z_{\beta A, \widehat{y}(m_T)}}{Z_{\beta A,0}}
        \\
        \frac{d p^s_T}{d \widehat p_T} (m) &= 
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{w_T}\mathds{1}_{S_T} \mid m_T=m] \cdot 
        \frac{e^{-\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(0,0)}}{e^{-\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(m, \widehat{y}(m))}} \cdot e^{-\frac{nT}{2}} \cdot \frac{Z_{\beta A, \widehat y(m)}}{Z_{\beta A,0}}
    \end{align*}
    where $w_T$ is defined by 
    \begin{align*}
dw_t &= \omega(m_t)\,dt, & 
\omega(m):&= \frac{1}{2} \left[ {\mathsf{Tr}(\widehat{Q}(m)) + \left\Vert {m}\right\Vert^2} \right].
    \end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- prf-5: body_md -->
We rederive the calculations from \cite[\S3.2]{davies2026potential}, but taking $m=m^y_t$ instead of assuming there is a unique TAP solution $m=\widehat{m}(y_t)$. Given that $(m_t,y_t)$ is an It\^o process satisfying \eqref{e:TAP} for all $t$, by It\^o's formula,
\begin{align*}
    d\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(m_t,y_t) &=
    \underbrace{\left\langle\nabla_m\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(m_t,y_t), dm_t\right\rangle}_{0}
    - \left\langlem_t,dy_t\right\rangle
    + \frac{1}{2} \mathsf{Tr}\left( {\nabla_m^2 \mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(m_t,y_t) d[y]_t} \right)
    - \mathsf{Tr}(d[m,y]_t).
\end{align*}
When $y_t$ satisfies \eqref{e:SL}, letting $m^y_t$ be the solution to \eqref{e:TAP}, 
\begin{align*}
    d\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(m_t^y,y_t) &= 
    -\left\langlem_t^y, dy_t\right\rangle + \frac{1}{2} \mathsf{Tr}\left( {\nabla_m^2 \mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(m_t,y_t) \widehat{Q}(m_t^y) \widehat{Q}(m_t^y)^\top } \right)dt
    - \mathsf{Tr}(\widehat{Q}(m^y_t))\\
    &= -\left\langle\widehat m^y_t,dy_t\right\rangle - \frac{1}{2} \mathsf{Tr}(\widehat{Q}(m^y_t))\,dt.
\end{align*}
Let $\mathbf{W}_T$ denote the Wiener measure. 
We consider the stopped ASL process defined by 
\[
d\widehat{y}_t = \mathds{1}_{t<\tau} \widehat{m}_t \,dt + dB_t,
\]
and denote the path measure by $\widehat \mathbf{P}_T^y$.
By Girsanov's Theorem,
\begin{align*}
    \frac{d \widehat{\mathbf{P}}_T^y}{d \mathbf{W}_T}(y_{[0,T]}) &= \exp\left( {
\int_0^{T\wedge \tau}
\left\langlem^y_t, dy_t\right\rangle - \frac{1}{2}\int_0^{T\wedge \tau} \left\Vert {m^y_t}\right\Vert^2\,dt
    } \right).
\end{align*}
Then
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \mathds{1}_{S_T}[m^y_{[0,T]}] e^{\widehat{w}_T(m^y_{[0,T]})} \frac{d \widehat{\mathbf{P}}_T^y}{d \mathbf{W}_T}(y_{[0,T]})
    &= \mathds{1}_{S_T}\exp\left( {\int_0^T \frac{1}{2} \left( {\mathsf{Tr}(\widehat{Q}(m^y_t)) + \left\Vert {m^y_t}\right\Vert^2} \right)\,dt} \right)\\
    &= \mathds{1}_{S_T}\exp\left( {-\int_0^T d\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(m_t^y,y_t)} \right)= \exp(\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(0,0) - \mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(m_T^y, y_T)).
\end{align*}
Define the survival event on paths $y_{[0,T]}$ to be that $m^y_{t}\in \mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$ for $t\leqslant T$.
Therefore, 
\[
\frac{d \widehat\mathbf{P}^{y,s}_T}{d \mathbf{W}^s_T}(y_{[0,T]}) = e^{-\widehat{w}_T(m_{[0,T]}^y)} \frac{e^{- \mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(m^y_T, y_T)}}{e^{-\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(0,0)}}.
\]
The SL process is given by $y_t = t\sigma + B_t$, i.e., $dy_t = \sigma\,dt + dB_t$, where $\sigma\sim \mu_{\beta A}$. By Girsanov's Theorem, conditioned on $\sigma$,
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^y(\cdot\mid \sigma)}{d \mathbf{W}_T} (y_{[0,T]})
    &= \exp\left( {\int_0^T\left\langle\sigma, dy_t\right\rangle - \frac{1}{2} \int_0^T \left\Vert {\sigma}\right\Vert^2\,dt} \right) 
    = \exp\left( {\left\langle\sigma,y_T\right\rangle - \frac{nT}{2}} \right).
\end{align*}
Averaging over $\sigma\sim \mu_{\beta A}$ then gives
\begin{align*}
    \frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^y}{d \mathbf{W}_T}(y_{[0,T]}) &= \sum_{\sigma\in \{\pm 1\}^n} 
    \frac{\exp\left( {\frac{\beta}{2} \left\langle\sigma, A\sigma\right\rangle} \right)}{Z_{\beta A, 0}} \exp\left( {\left\langle\sigma,y_T\right\rangle - \frac{nT}{2}} \right) = e^{-\frac{nT}{2}} \frac{Z_{\beta A, y_T}}{Z_{\beta A, 0}}.
\end{align*}
Note this only depends on the endpoint of the path. We have that $\frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^s}{d \mathbf{W}_T^s}$ equals the same expression. Then
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^{y,s}}{d \widehat{\mathbf{P}}_T^{y,s}}
    &= \frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^{y,s}}{d \mathbf{W}_T^s}\Big/
     \frac{d \widehat{\mathbf{P}}_T^{y,s}}{d \mathbf{W}_T^s}
    = e^{\widehat{w}_T(m^y_{[0,T]})}\cdot 
        \frac{e^{-\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(0,0)}}{e^{-\mathcal F_{\,\mathrm{TAP}}(m^y_T, y_T)}} \cdot e^{-\frac{nT}{2}} \cdot \frac{Z_{\beta A, y_T}}{Z_{\beta A,0}}.
\end{align*}
Pushing forward to $m$-space via $y_{[0,T]}\mapsto m^y_{[0,T]}$, multiplying by $\mathds{1}_{S_T}$, and taking a conditional expectation with respect to the endpoint $m^y_T$ then gives the second result.
<!-- end prf-5 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{l:my-safe}
Suppose that \ref{d:Q-error}, \ref{d:safe-set-stay-inside}, \ref{d:Q-reg}, \ref{d:drift-error}, and \ref{d:Q-Lip} hold to time $T$.
    Let $y_t$ solve (SL). 
    Define $\mathfrak S_t$ on the magnetization path space corresponding to $\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$. 
    Then for all $t\in [0,T]$,
    \[
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}[m^y_t\nin \mathfrak S_t] = o_n(1).
    \]
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- prf-6: body_md -->
Abbreviate $\widetilde m_t= m^y_t$. We consider the mismatch between the true magnetization and the magnetization estimated from following TAP equation,
\begin{align*}
    dy_t &= m_t \,dt + dB_t\\
    dm_t &= Q(m_t)\,dB_t\\
    d\widetilde m_t &= \widehat{Q}(\widetilde m_t) (dy_t - f(\widetilde m_t)\,dt)= \widehat{Q}(\widetilde m_t) ((m_t- f(\widetilde m_t)) \,dt + dB_t).
\end{align*}
Let $\widetilde \Delta_t= m_t - \widetilde m_t$. We have
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
d\widetilde \Delta_t &= 
[\underbrace{\widehat{Q}(\widetilde m_t)(m_t - \widetilde m_t)}_{b^{\mathrm{Lip}}} + \underbrace{\widehat{Q}(\widetilde m_t)(\widetilde m_t - f(\widetilde m_t))}_{b^{\mathrm{err}}}]\,dt + 
[\underbrace{(\widehat{Q}(m_t) - \widehat{Q}(\widetilde m_t))}_{C^{\mathrm{Lip}}} + \underbrace{(Q(m_t) - \widehat{Q}(m_t))}_{C^{\mathrm{err}}}] \,dB_t
\end{align*}
Note
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \left\Vert {b_t^{\mathrm{Lip}}}\right\Vert &\leqslant c^{-1}\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert & \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{t<\tau}\left\Vert {b_t^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert^2} \right] &\leqslant c^{-2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}^2\\
    \left\Vert {C_t^{\mathrm{Lip}}}\right\Vert_F &\leqslant L\left\Vert {\Delta_t}\right\Vert & 
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{t<\tau}\left\Vert {C_t^{\mathrm{err}}}\right\Vert_F^2} \right]&\leqslant \varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}}^2.
\end{align*}
Then \prettyref{l:safe-pair} gives that (defining the safe sets on just the magnetization path space)
\[
\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}[\widetilde m_{[0,t]} \nin \mathfrak S_t]\leqslant 
\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}[(m_{[0,t]}, \widetilde m_{[0,t]})\nin \mathfrak S^2_t(\rho\sqrt{n})] = o_n(1).
\]
<!-- end prf-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{l:dual-phd-bounded}
Assume \ref{d:drift-error}. Then the following hold within the domain $\{x\in\mathbb R^n:\tanh(x)\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)\}$:
\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=0.1em]
\item \label{i:lip-hS}
$\ensuremath{\left\lVert \widehat\Sigma \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}} \leqslant c^{-1}$.
\item \label{i:lip-hfs}
$\ensuremath{\left\lVert {\widehat f^*} \right\rVert_{2}} \leqslant c^{-2}\sqrt{n}$.
\item With $m = \tanh(x)$, $\ensuremath{\left\lVert {D(m)\widehat Q(m)(m-f(m))} \right\rVert_{2}} \leqslant c^{-1}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}$
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
These follow directly from the bound on $D(m)\widehat{Q}(m)$ given by \prettyref{def:good-points}, the bound $\ensuremath{\left\lVert {m} \right\rVert_{2}} \leqslant \sqrt{n}$ implied by $m \in (-1,1)^n$, and the bound on $\ensuremath{\left\lVert {m - f(m)} \right\rVert_{2}}$ given by \ref{d:drift-error}.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:stay-in-cube}
Assume $\ensuremath{\left\lVert A \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}} \leqslant 3$, \ref{d:drift-error}, and $0^n\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$ for $c > 0$.
Then the primal and dual equations \eqref{eq:asl-tap-m}--\eqref{eq:phd-m}
and \eqref{eq:d-PHD}--\eqref{eq:d-ASL-TAP} have pathwise unique strong
solutions up to their respective first exits from the good set $\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$ or its
dual-coordinate image $\tanh^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c))$. Their stopped solutions are defined for all
$t\geqslant0$, and almost surely
\[
            \widehat m_{t\wedge\tau_c}\in(-1,1)^n
                           \qquad(t<\infty).
\]
 
The ASL tilt and JE weight also have finite limits at a finite exit and
can be stopped there.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Write either dual equation as
$d\widehat x_t=b(\widehat x_t)\,dt+\widehat\Sigma(\widehat x_t)\,dB_t$ on
$U:=\tanh^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c))$, with $\widehat x_0=0$.
Its coefficients are smooth on $U$. Then \prettyref{l:dual-phd-bounded} gives
\[
    \sup_{x\in U}\ensuremath{\left\lVert {b(x)} \right\rVert_{2}}\leqslant \infty,
    \qquad
    \sup_{x\in U}\ensuremath{\left\lVert \widehat\Sigma(x) \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}\leqslant \infty.
\]
With $\operatorname{dist}$ being the distance between a point and a set, define
\[
    U_k:=\{x\in U:\ensuremath{\left\lVert {x} \right\rVert_{2}}<k,\
                  \operatorname{dist}(x,U^c)>k^{-1}\}.
\]
For $k$ large enough that $0\in U_k$, use
$\overline U_k\subseteq U_{k+1}$ to choose
$\chi_k\in C_c^\infty(U_{k+1})$ with $0\leqslant\chi_k\leqslant1$ and
$\chi_k=1$ on $\overline U_k$.
The coefficient pairs $(\chi_k b,\chi_k\widehat\Sigma)$, extended by zero
outside $U$, are globally Lipschitz, so
\cite[Theorem 5.2.1]{oksendal2003stochastic} gives global strong solutions
$\widehat x^{(k)}$ with these coefficients, the same Brownian motion, and
initial value $0$.
Set $\tau_k:=\inf\{t:\widehat x_t^{(k)}\notin U_k\}$.
The stopped uniqueness argument in that theorem gives, for $\ell\geqslant k$,
\[
    \tau_k\leqslant\tau_\ell,\qquad
    \widehat x^{(\ell)}_{t\wedge\tau_k}
      =\widehat x^{(k)}_{t\wedge\tau_k}.
\]
Thus $\tau_c:=\lim_k\tau_k$ and
$\widehat x_t:=\widehat x_t^{(k)}$ whenever $t<\tau_k$ define the pathwise
unique solution before $\tau_c$.

Taking the integrands to be zero for $s\geqslant\tau_c$, we have
$\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_B\int_0^T\mathbf1_{\{s<\tau_c\}}
\|\widehat\Sigma(\widehat x_s)\|_F^2\,ds\leqslant C_{\beta,c}^2nT$.
Hence It\^o isometry, \cite[Theorem 3.2.5]{oksendal2003stochastic},
and the drift bound give the continuous extension
\[
    \widehat x_{t\wedge\tau_c}
    :=\int_0^t\mathbf1_{\{s<\tau_c\}}b(\widehat x_s)\,ds
      +\int_0^t\mathbf1_{\{s<\tau_c\}}\widehat\Sigma(\widehat x_s)\,dB_s,
    \qquad t<\infty.
\]
On $\{\tau_c<\infty\}$, continuity gives $\widehat x_{\tau_k}\in\partial U_k$, so
\[
    \ensuremath{\left\lVert {\widehat x_{\tau_k}} \right\rVert_{2}}=k
    \quad\text{or}\quad
    \operatorname{dist}(\widehat x_{\tau_k},U^c)=k^{-1}.
\]
Since $\widehat x_{\tau_k}\to\widehat x_{\tau_c}\in\mathbb R^n$, the first alternative is impossible for large $k$. Thus $\widehat x_{\tau_c}\in\partial U$, and $\tau_c$ is the first exit from $U$.

It\^o's formula applied to $\tanh$ and locally to $\tanh^{-1}$ as in \cite[Lemma 6.9]{davies2026potential}, gives the primal equations, pathwise uniqueness, and $\widehat m_{t\wedge\tau_c}=\tanh(\widehat x_{t\wedge\tau_c})\in(-1,1)^n$.
Finally, for $t<\tau_c$, recover
\[
    \widehat y_t=\int_0^t\widehat m_s\,ds+B_t,\qquad
    \widehat w_t=\frac12\int_0^t
       \bigl(\mathsf{Tr}\widehat Q(\widehat m_s)+\ensuremath{\left\lVert {\widehat m_s} \right\rVert_{2}}^2\bigr)\,ds.
\]
Since $\ensuremath{\left\lVert {\widehat m_s} \right\rVert_{2}}\leqslant\sqrt n$ and $0\leqslant\mathsf{Tr}\widehat Q(\widehat m_s)\leqslant n/c$ before exit, both have finite limits at a finite $\tau_c$ and can be stopped there.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
\label{def:Tp-transport-inequality}
A probability measure $P$ on a metric space satisfies a $T_p$ transportation inequality with constant $C$ if $d_{W,p}(P,\mu)^2\leqslant2C H(\mu\mid P)$ for every probability measure $\mu$, where $d_{W,p}$ denotes Wasserstein distance and $H$ relative
entropy.
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:t1-subgaussian}
If $P$ satisfies $T_1$ with constant $C$, then every integrable
$K_F$-Lipschitz function $F$ satisfies
\[
 \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_P [e^{\lambda(F-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_P[F])}]
       \leqslant e^{\lambda^2CK_F^2/2}\quad(\forall \lambda\in\mathbb R),\qquad
 P\{|F-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_P[F]|>r\}\leqslant2e^{-r^2/(2CK_F^2)}\quad(\forall r>0).
\]
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:t2-transport-SDE}
Let $C,K,T>0$. Consider the global solution of $dX_t=b(X_t)\,dt+\sigma(X_t)\,dB_t$, $X_0=0$, on $[0,T]$.
Suppose that $\ensuremath{\left\lVert \sigma(x) \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}\leqslant C$ for all $x\in\mathbb R^n$, that $b$ is $K$-Lipschitz in Euclidean norm, and that $\sigma$ is
$K/\sqrt n$-Lipschitz in $\ensuremath{\left\lVert {\cdot} \right\rVert_{L^{2}}}$.
Then its path law on $C^0([0,T],\mathbb R^n)$ with uniform Euclidean distance satisfies $T_2$ with constant $3TC^2\exp(3T(T+4)K^2)$.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
This is the time-homogeneous special case of \cite[Theorem 6.12]{davies2026potential}.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:mSDE-path-subgaussian}
Assume $\ensuremath{\left\lVert A \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}} \leqslant 3$, \ref{d:drift-error}, \ref{d:Q-Lip}, and $0^n\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$ for $c > 0$. Let $0<T<\infty$.
Then there is a global strong solution
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:alg-global-extension}
 d\widehat m_t^{\mathrm{ext}}=b_{\mathrm{ext}}(\widehat m_t^{\mathrm{ext}})\,dt
              +\sigma_{\mathrm{ext}}(\widehat m_t^{\mathrm{ext}})\,dB_t,
                    \qquad \widehat m_0^{\mathrm{ext}}=0,
\end{equation}
whose coefficients agree on $\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$ with
$\widehat Q(m)(m-f(m))$ and $\widehat Q(m)$, respectively.
Driven by the same Brownian motion, it agrees with the original ASL-TAP process up to their common first exit from $\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$.
Let $P$ be its path law on $\mathcal W=C^0([0,T],\mathbb R^n)$ with the uniform norm $\|x\|_\infty=\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant T}\ensuremath{\left\lVert {x_t} \right\rVert_{2}}$.
For a constant $K$ depending on $L$ and $L_{\mathrm{drift}}$, set
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:alg-T2-constant}
            c_{T_2}:=3Tc^{-2}\exp\!\left(3T(T+4)K^2\right).
\end{equation}
Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},itemsep=0.3em]
\item For every probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathcal W$,
$d_{W,2}(P,\mu)^2\leqslant2c_{T_2}H(\mu\mid P)$.
\item For every $P$-integrable $K_F$-Lipschitz functional $F$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:alg-global-MGF}
 \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_P [e^{\lambda(F-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_P[F])}]
       \leqslant\exp\!\left(\frac{\lambda^2c_{T_2}K_F^2}{2}\right)
                                      \qquad(\forall \lambda\in\mathbb R).
\end{equation}
In sub-Gaussian tail-bound form,
$P\{|F-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_P[F]|>r\}\leqslant2\exp(-r^2/(2c_{T_2}K_F^2))$ for $r>0$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Follow \cite[Theorem 6.13]{davies2026potential}, extending from
$\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$ rather than from the whole cube.
By \ref{d:Q-Lip}, Kirszbraun's theorem gives
extensions satisfying
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:alg-extended-coefficients}
 \|\sigma_{\mathrm{ext}}(x)-\sigma_{\mathrm{ext}}(y)\|_F
       \leqslant K\ensuremath{\left\lVert {x-y} \right\rVert_{2}},\qquad
 \ensuremath{\left\lVert {b_{\mathrm{ext}}(x)-b_{\mathrm{ext}}(y)} \right\rVert_{2}}
       \leqslant K\ensuremath{\left\lVert {x-y} \right\rVert_{2}}.
\end{equation}
Project the diffusion extension in Frobenius norm onto the closed convex set $\{Q\in\mathrm{Sym}_n:0\preceq Q\preceq c^{-1}I_n\}$.
This preserves its Lipschitz constant and its values on the good set.
Global strong existence follows, and pathwise uniqueness identifies the paths until exit. 
Apply \prettyref{thm:t2-transport-SDE} with $C=c^{-1}$ and the above $K$. 
As in \cite[Theorem 6.13]{davies2026potential}, $d_{W,1}\leqslant d_{W,2}$ then implies $T_1$, and \prettyref{thm:t1-subgaussian} gives (ii).
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:indicator-MGF}
Let $F$ be a real random variable with
$\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} [e^{s(F-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F])}]\leqslant e^{vs^2/2}$ for all $s\in\mathbb R$, for some $v>0$.
Then for any event $S$ and every $\lambda\in\mathbb R$,
\[
 \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\!\left[\mathbf1_S e^{\lambda(F-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F\mid S])}\right]
                              \leqslant e^{2v\lambda^2}.
\]
If $\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}(S)=0$, then the left-hand side is defined to be zero.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Let $q=\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}(S) > 0$ since the $q=0$ case holds by convention.
Conditional Jensen's inequality and the MGF hypothesis at $s=-2\lambda$
give
\[
    q\,e^{-2\lambda(\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F\mid S]-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F])}
    \leqslant q\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{-2\lambda(F - \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F])}\mid S] = \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\!\left[\mathbf1_S e^{-2\lambda(F-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F])}\right]
    \leqslant e^{2v\lambda^2}.
\]
Taking square roots and then applying Cauchy--Schwarz, we obtain
\begin{align*}
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\!\left[\mathbf1_S e^{\lambda(F-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F\mid S])}\right]
    &=
    e^{\lambda(\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F]-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F\mid S])}
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\!\left[\mathbf1_S e^{\lambda(F-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F])}\right]\\
    &\leqslant
    \bigl(q\,e^{-2\lambda(\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F\mid S]-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F])}\bigr)^{1/2}
    \bigl(\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{2\lambda(F-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[F])}]\bigr)^{1/2}\\
    &\leqslant e^{v\lambda^2}e^{v\lambda^2}
     =e^{2v\lambda^2},
\end{align*}
where the last step also uses the MGF hypothesis at $s=2\lambda$.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:JE-subgaussian}
    Suppose $0^n\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$ and assume \ref{d:drift-error}, \ref{d:Q-Lip}, and \ref{d:JE-lip}.
    Stop the original ASL-TAP-JE process at
$\tau_c$, and set $S_T=\{\tau_c>T\}$.
Then for every $0<T<\infty$ and $\lambda\in\mathbb R$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:alg-killed-JE-MGF}
 \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_B\!\left[\mathbf1_{S_T}
    e^{\lambda(\widehat w_T-\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_B[\widehat w_T\mid S_T])}\right]
      \leqslant\exp\!\left(2\lambda^2c_{T_2}T^2L_\omega^2\right),
\end{equation}
where $c_{T_2}$ is as in \prettyref{thm:mSDE-path-subgaussian} and
$L_\omega$ as in \ref{d:JE-lip}.
The left-hand side is zero by convention if $\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_B(S_T)=0$.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Follow the path-functional argument in
\cite[Corollary 6.14(d5)]{davies2026potential}, using
\ref{d:JE-lip} instead of integrating a gradient along
the whole cube, and extend $\omega$ to all of $\mathbb R^n$, for example by
\[
 \omega_{\mathrm{ext}}(x):=
       \inf_{m\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)}
             \{\omega(m)+L_\omega\ensuremath{\left\lVert {x-m} \right\rVert_{2}}\}.
\]
This agrees with $\omega$ on the good set and is $L_\omega$-Lipschitz.
The path functional $F(x):=\int_0^T\omega_{\mathrm{ext}}(x_t)\,dt$
is $TL_\omega$-Lipschitz and integrable for the global law in
\prettyref{thm:mSDE-path-subgaussian}, so \eqref{eq:alg-global-MGF} gives a sub-Gaussian MGF with
variance proxy $v=c_{T_2}T^2L_\omega^2$ for $F(\widehat m^{\mathrm{ext}})$ on the extended SDE.
On $S_T$, the original and extended paths agree, hence
$F(\widehat m^{\mathrm{ext}})=\widehat w_T$ and their surviving-path means coincide, so
finally, apply \prettyref{lem:indicator-MGF} to remove paths outside of $S_T$ from the bound.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{l:je-rs}
    Suppose that  
    $f_t$ and $\widehat f_t$ are such that pathwise unique strong solutions to the following SDE's exist:
    \begin{align}
        & dx_t = f_t\, dt + dB_t&& x_0\sim  p_0\\
        \label{e:je-generic}
        & 
        d\widehat{x}_t = \widehat{f}_t\, dt+ dB_t 
        &&  
        \widehat{x}_0\sim  p_0.  
    \end{align}
    Let $\mathcal S\subseteq \mathbb R^n$ and for a process $\gamma$ adapted to $B_t$, define the stopping time $\tau(\gamma):= \inf_{t\geqslant 0} \left\{{t}:{\gamma_t\nin \mathcal S}\right\}$. 
    Let $\mathbf{P}_T,\widehat\mathbf{P}_T$ be the path measures for $x_{[0,T]},\widehat{x}_{[0,T]}$ and $\mathbf{P}_T^s, \widehat\mathbf{P}_T^s$ be the path measures for $x_{[0,T]}, \widehat{x}_{[0,T]}$ conditioned on survival, $S_T = \{\tau > T\}$.
    Let $ p_t, \widehat p_t$ be the density of $x_t, \widehat x_t$, respectively and $p_t^s$, $\widehat{p}_t^s$ be the densities for $x_t$, $\widehat x_t$, restricted to surviving paths $S_t$. 
    Suppose that $\widehat{w}_t(\widehat{x}_{[0,T]})$ is defined so that 
    \[
\frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^s}{d \widehat{\mathbf{P}}_T} (\gamma) = \mathds{1}_{S_T}(\gamma) \frac{e^{\widehat w_T(\gamma)}}{\widehat Z_T} \frac{R_2(\gamma_T)}{Z_2}
    \]
    where $\widehat Z_t = \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[e^{\widehat{w}_t}\mid S_t]$ 
    and so $\frac{d p_T^s}{d \widehat p_T}(x) = \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{S_T}(\widehat{x}_{[0,T]}) e^{\widehat w_T}/\widehat Z_T \mid \widehat{x}_T=x] \frac{R_2(x)}{Z_2}$.
    Here, $\widehat{w}_t=\widehat{w}_t(\widehat{x}_{[0,t]})$ unless specified otherwise.
    Suppose with $L=\exp\left( {\frac{3}{\varepsilon}\left( {\frac{1}{2} \int_0^T \varepsilon(t)^2dt+1} \right)} \right)$,
    \begin{enumerate}
    \item[(0)] (Survival probability) $\mathbf{P}_T(S_T) \geqslant 1-\delta$ and $\widehat \mathbf{P}_T(S_T) \geqslant 1-\widehat \delta$.
        \item (Drift error) For a stopping time $\tau'$ such that $\tau'\leqslant \tau$, we have
        $\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{x_{[0,T]}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{\{t<\tau'\}}\left\Vert {\widehat{f}_t-f_t}\right\Vert^2} \right]\leqslant \varepsilon(t)^2$ and $\mathbf{P}_T[\tau'> T]\leqslant \delta'$.
        \item (Exponential upper tail for log-weights)
        $\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{S_T}(\widehat{x}_{[0,T]}) e^{\widehat{w}_T}\mathds{1}_{e^{\widehat{w}_T}/\widehat Z_T\geqslant C_1}} \right]\leqslant \varepsilon_1 \widehat Z_T$, for $\varepsilon_1 = \frac{c_3\varepsilon}{3 L}$.
        \item (Lower tail for log-weights)
        $\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left[ {\{\widehat{x}_{[0,T]}\in S_T\}\cap \left\{ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T}\leqslant c_3} \right\}} \right]\leqslant \varepsilon_3$, for $\varepsilon_3 = \frac{\varepsilon}{3 L}$. 
    \end{enumerate}
    Then $\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {
            \frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T} \frac{d  p_T}{d \rho_T}(\widehat{x}_T)\wedge \frac{2C_1L}{c_3}} \right]\geqslant 1-\varepsilon-\delta$, and given query access to $R_2(x)$, we can sample from $ p_T$ with TV error $2\varepsilon +  
            \delta + \delta'$ by $O\left( {\frac{C_1^2L^2\log\left( {\frac{1}{\varepsilon}} \right)}{c_3^2}} \right)$ simulations of \eqref{e:je-generic}, provided that $2\varepsilon+\delta<\frac{1}{2}$, by choosing the parameters  $C_1'=C_2'=\frac{C_1L}{c_3}$ and using the estimate 
            $e^{\widehat{w}_T} R_2(\widehat{x}_T)$
            in \prettyref{a:ars}. 
Suppose moreover that  
$\widetilde{x}_{[0,T]}$
is a random process and  
$\widetilde{w}_T(x_{[0,T]})$ are weights such that 
\begin{enumerate}[resume]
    \item (Path error) \label{i:je-path-error}
    $\operatorname{TV}(\widehat \mathbf{P}_T^s, \widetilde{\mathbf{P}}_T^s) \leqslant \varepsilon_4$ for some $\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}_T^s$ such that $\frac{d \widetilde{\mathbf{P}}_T^s}{d \widetilde{\mathbf{P}}_T}\leqslant 1$, where $\varepsilon_4 = \frac{\varepsilon}{384C_1^{\prime2}}$.\footnote{We define TV error for measures which may not be probability measures in the same way, $\operatorname{TV}(P,Q) = \sup_A |P(A)-Q(A)|$.}
    \item (Weight error) \label{i:je-ewt}
    $\widetilde \mathbf{P}_T^s \left( {|\widetilde{w}_T - \widehat{w}_T|> \frac{\varepsilon_4}{2}} \right)\leqslant \frac{\varepsilon_4}{2}$.
    \item (Ratio error) \label{i:je-ert}
    $\widetilde R_2(x)$ is a (randomized) estimate such that  
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
(a) &&\forall x,\quad  \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left( {\frac{\widetilde R_2(x_T)}{R_2(x_T)}\nin [0, e^{\varepsilon_4/2}] \Big| x_T=x} \right) &\leqslant \frac{\varepsilon_4}{2}\\
(b)&&    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left( {\frac{\widetilde R_2(x_T)}{R_2(x_T)}\nin [e^{-\varepsilon_4/2}, e^{\varepsilon_4/2}]} \right)&\leqslant \varepsilon
\end{align*}
\end{enumerate}
Then we can sample from $p_T$ with TV error $6\varepsilon+\delta + \delta'+ 768{C_1'}^2\widehat \delta$ by $O\left( {\frac{C_1^2L^2\log\left( {\frac{1}{\varepsilon}} \right)}{c_3^2}} \right)$ simulations of \eqref{e:je-generic} using the estimate  
$e^{\widetilde{w}_T(\widetilde x_{ [0,T]})} \widetilde R_2(\widetilde{x}_T)$
            in \prettyref{a:ars}, provided that this error is $<\frac{1}{2}$.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- prf-7: body_md -->
We check the conditions of \prettyref{l:je-rs} where the law $\mathbf{P}_T$ of the ideal process $m_t$ is given by \eqref{e:HD}, and the law of the approximate process $\widehat \mathbf{P}_T$ is given by \eqref{e:ASL-TAP} with weights \eqref{e:JE}. The algorithm computes the Euler-Maruyama discretization with some step size $h$ to be determined:
\begin{align*}
    \widetilde{y}_{t+h} &= \widetilde{y}_t + h \widetilde{m}_t + \sqrt{h}\xi_t, \quad \xi_t \sim \mathcal N(0,I_n), & \widetilde{y}_0&=0\\
    \widetilde{w}_{t+h} &= \widetilde{w}_t + \frac{h}{2} \left[ {\mathsf{Tr}(\widehat{Q}(\widetilde m_t)) + \left\Vert {\widetilde m_t}\right\Vert^2} \right]. 
\end{align*}
We first consider the case where $\widetilde m_t$ is computed exactly from $\widetilde y_t$ from \eqref{e:TAP}.
We take $\mathcal S = \mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)$, so that $\tau=\inf \left\{{t\geqslant 0}:{m_t\nin \mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)}\right\}$ and $S_T = \{\forall t\in [0,T], \, m_t \in \mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)\}$. We take 
\[\tau' = \inf\left\{{t\geqslant 0}:{((m_t,y_t),(\widehat{m}_t,\widehat{y}_t))\nin \mathcal S^2(\rho\sqrt n , c\rho\sqrt n/2)}\right\},\] 
where the expanded pair safe set is defined with $\mathcal S=\left\{{(m,y)}:{m\in \mathcal{S}_{\beta, A}(c)}\right\}$. The Radon-Nikodym derivative is in the form specified by the lemma, by \prettyref{l:je-survival}.

 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Part 0 (survival probability).}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 Note that $\mathbf{P}_T(S_T^c) = o_n(1)$ directly from \ref{d:safe-set-stay-inside}, while $\mathbf{P}_T(S_T^c) = o_n(1)$ follows from \prettyref{l:my-safe}.

 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Part 1 (drift error).}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 This holds with $\varepsilon(t) = O(1)$ by \prettyref{l:m-error}. 

 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Part 2 \& 3 (tails for log-weights).}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 
This follows from the subgaussianity of Jarzynski weights, \prettyref{cor:JE-subgaussian} by \cite[Lemma 3.10]{davies2026potential}, applied to the measure conditional on survival. 

 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Part 4 (path error).}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 
Discretization error in \cite{davies2026potential} is analyzed in path space, using Lipschitzness of a uniquely defined $\widehat{m}(y)$ solving \eqref{e:TAP}. Instead, we can conduct the analysis on a lift of $y$-space $\widehat y(\mathcal S(c))$ based on homotopy equivalence in $\widehat y(\mathcal S(c))$; then $\widehat{m}$ is well-defined and Lipschitz on the lifted space. With $O\left( {\frac{1}{n}} \right)$ step size, Brownian excursions are $o(1)$ with high probability, so when $\widehat{m}$ lies within the expanded safe set, all arguments can be done within a single branch.
We find that it suffices to take $O\left( {\frac{\varepsilon_4^2}{n}} \right)$ step size with an appropriate constant to obtain TV error $\varepsilon_4$.


 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Part 5 (weight error).}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 Following the discretization arguments in \cite{davies2026potential} with the above remarks, to obtain the needed error bound, it suffices to take $h=O\left( {\frac{\varepsilon_4^2}{n^2}} \right)$ with an appropriate constant.

 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Part 6 (ratio error).}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 
We need to check the estimation of $\widehat Z_{\beta A,y}$. 
With high probability over $A$, we can estimate the partition function on any small enough Hamming ball $B(\sigma, c(\beta)n)\in \{\pm1\}^n$, by using the sampling guarantee for all temperatures $\beta'\leqslant \beta$ (\ref{thm:localized-sampler}) along with simulated annealing, as in \cite[\S7.5]{davies2026potential}; this gives an underestimate with high probability not depending on $\sigma$.
When $\sigma = \operatorname{sign}(y_T)$ where $y_T$ is the SL tilt, for large enough $T=T(\beta)$, with probability $1-e^{-\Omega(n)}$, this wedge contains all but $e^{-\Omega(n)}$ of the mass of the entire partition function, by \cite[Lemma 7.43]{davies2026potential}. 

 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Putting everything together.}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 Thus by \prettyref{l:je-rs}, the output of Part 1 of \prettyref{alg:main} is a sample $\widetilde{y}_T\sim \widetilde  p_T$ with $\operatorname{TV}(\widetilde p_T,  p_T)=o_n(1)$, in $\mathsf{poly}(n)$ time. 
By \prettyref{thm:localized-sampler},  
with $1-o_n(1)$ probability over $y_T\sim  p_T$, we obtain a sample $\widehat \sigma$ that is $o_n(1)$ in TV distance from $ \mu_{\beta A, y_T}$ in polynomial time. 

Letting $\widetilde \mu$ be the distribution of $\widehat\sigma$ for tilt $\widetilde{y}_T$, we have by the chain rule for TV distance that
\begin{align*}
    \operatorname{TV}(\widetilde \mu, \mu)
    &\leqslant 
    \operatorname{TV}(\widetilde  p_T,  p_T) + 
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{y_T\sim  p_T} \operatorname{TV} (\mathcal L(\widehat\sigma | y_T), \mathcal L(\sigma | y_T) ) = o_n(1),
\end{align*}
as needed.

 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Error in solving \eqref{e:TAP}.}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 Robustness to approximating the solution to \eqref{e:TAP} is shown in \cite[\S D]{davies2026potential}. The same mirror descent analysis works with initialization that is within a ball around the true solution $m$ that is inside the expanded safe set, which is ensured under Brownian excursions at every step being at most $O(1)$.
<!-- end prf-7 -->
<!-- desideratum-1: body_md -->
For every $0 \leqslant T(\beta) < \infty$, there exist $\rho(\beta, T), c_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(\beta,T) > 0$ such that
    \[ \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_{A,B_t}\left\{\forall t \in [0,T] \;.\; B_{\square}(m_t, \rho\sqrt{n}) \subseteq \mathcal S_A(c_{{\mathrm{TAP}}})\right\} \geqslant 1 - o_n(1)\,. \]
<!-- end desideratum-1 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:hybrid-local-convexity}
    For every $\beta, t > 0$, there exist $c_0(\beta,t), c_1(\beta,t) > 0$, such that, for all $0 < \varepsilon < c_1(\beta,t)$ and $k \geqslant k_0(\varepsilon,\beta,t)$,
    \[
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_{A, g_0}\left\{\inf_{\sigma \in B_\square(\overline{m}_{k-1},\varepsilon\sqrt{n})} \nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(\sigma,\cdot) \succeq c_0(\beta,t)\right\} \geqslant 1 - o_n(1)\,.
    \]
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- prf-8: body_md -->
The proof is exactly analogous to \cite[Theorem 2]{celentano2024sudakov} with the small modification that it tests the quadratic forms of the TAP Hessian considered in our previous work \cite{davies2026potential}\footnote{\,This is equivalent to the Hessian used by Fan, Mei and Montanari \cite{fan2021tap}.} evaluated at the AMP iterates given above. Using gauge symmetry, we fix $x_0=\mathbf{1}^n$. Throughout the proof, the AMP law is the one corresponding to the iterates which come from \cite[Algorithm 1, (1)--(5)]{el2022sampling} and the Hessian is $\nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(\sigma,\cdot)$\footnote{\,The original result of Celentano \cite[Theorems 1--2]{celentano2024sudakov} tests AMP iterates designed by Fan et al \cite{fan2021tap} on the TAP Hessian $\nabla^2\mathcal F_{\mathrm{TAP}}(\sigma,\cdot)$ or the AMP iterates designed by El-Alaoui et al \cite{el2022sampling} on $\overline{Q}^{-1}(\cdot)$. We require testing the AMP iterates of Alaoui et al \cite{el2022sampling} on the TAP Hessian $\nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(\sigma,\cdot)$ -- this leads to the name ``hybrid'' local convexity.}. For completeness, we briefly recreate the proof of \cite[Theorem 2, Pg 11--18] {celentano2024sudakov} by analyzing the objective $-v^\mathsf{T}\nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot)v$ on the AMP transcript $(\overline{m}_0,\dots,\overline{m}_{k-1},g_0,g_1,\dots,g_k)$ and the limiting joint law $\mathcal S^{(2)}_1(\varepsilon) := (V,U,M,G,G_0,\Xi)$ where $\Xi \sim \mathcal N(0,1)$ (independently of $G$ and $G_0$), $V$ and $U$ are the empirical limiting empirical laws of the test vector $v \in B_\square(r,x)$ and the evaluation vector $u$ respectively, and the joint law satisfies the constraints in the definition of the set given in \cite[Pg 14]{celentano2024sudakov}.
     
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Reduction to the scalar objective.}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 For every $v \in \mathcal S^{n-1}(1)$, expanding the Hessian yields
    \allowdisplaybreaks
    \begin{align*}
        -\frac{1}{n}v^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(\sigma,\cdot)v
        &= \beta v^{\mathsf{T}}A v
           +\frac{\beta^2}{n}\langle\mathbf{1},v\rangle^2
           -\sum_{i=1}^n\frac{v_i^2}{1-u_i^2}
           -\lambda^2(1-Q(u))
           +\frac{2\lambda^2}{n}\langle u,v\rangle^2\,,
    \end{align*}
    which is just $f_{\text{FMM}}(v,u)$ as defined on \cite[Pg.\,11]{celentano2024sudakov}. We now apply the post-AMP comparison to the AMS transcript $(\overline{m}_0,\dots,\overline{m}_{k-1})$ and $f_{\text{FMM}}(v,u)$, followed by the empirical-law and fixed-point reductions \cite[Corollary\,1, Lemma\,1 and \S 4.2--4.3]{celentano2024sudakov}. These give
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:hybproof-reduction}
        \limsup_{\epsilon\downarrow0}\limsup_{k\to\infty}
        \mathop{\mathrm{p}\text{-}\limsup}_{n\to\infty}
        T_{n,k}(\epsilon)
        \leqslant
        \limsup_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}
        \sup_{\mu\in\mathcal S^{(2)}_1(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{F}^{(2)}_0(\mu)\,,
    \end{equation}
    where $T_{n,k}(\varepsilon) := \sup_{u \in B_\square(\overline{m}_{k-1},\varepsilon\sqrt{n}), \left\lVert{v}\right\rVert_2=1} -v^\mathsf{T}\widehat{Q}(u)v$ and $\mathcal F^{(2)}_0(\mu)$ is as defined in \cite[(24)]{celentano2024sudakov}. Carrying the computations in \cite[\S 4.4]{celentano2024sudakov} further by rewriting the Lagrange multiplier to combine with the AMS state evolution and FMM TAP Hesisan gives
    \[
         \sup_{\mu\in\mathcal S^{(2)}_1(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{F}^{(2)}_0(\mu) \leqslant \sup_{\rho,u \in [-1,1]}\min_{(\alpha_\rho,\alpha_u) \in K} \mathsf{L}(\rho,u;\alpha_\rho,\alpha_u,\alpha_v) + C_K\varepsilon\,,
    \]
    for some constant $C_K > 0$. Expanding the Lagrangian as in after \cite[(27)]{celentano2024sudakov} yields
    \[
        \sup_{\mu\in\mathcal S^{(2)}_1(\varepsilon)}\mathcal{F}^{(2)}_0(\mu) \leqslant \sup_{\rho,u \in [-1,1]} (\rho,u)^\mathsf{T} S_0(\alpha_v) (\rho,u) + C_K\varepsilon\,,
    \]
    where $S_0(\alpha_v) = A_{11,0}(\alpha_v) - A_{12}(\alpha_v)A_{22}(\alpha_v)^{-1}A_{12}(\alpha_v)^\mathsf{T}$ and these matrices are explicitly derived next\footnote{\,This is done for completeness, though the calculations in \cite[\S 4.4]{celentano2024sudakov} also cover our case almost entirely.}.

 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{The hybrid Schur-complement calculation.}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 We compute the blocks at $\alpha_v=0$. We first apply Stein's lemma over $G$ and keep $G_0$ fixed. Since the variance is $\beta^2q^*$, and we have $\partial_GM=1-M^2$, together with the Nishimori moment identities ($m^* = q^*$ and \prettyref{lem:nishimori-third}), this gives
    \allowdisplaybreaks
    \begin{align*}
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[G M(1-M^2)] &=\beta^2q^*\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[(1-M^2)(1-3M^2)] =\beta^2q^*\,d_0\,,\\
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[G(1-M^2)] &=-2\beta^2q\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[M(1-M^2)] =-2\beta^2q^*(q^*-b^*)\,,\\
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[G^2(1-M^2)] &=\beta^2q^*\,\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[1-M^2-2GM(1-M^2)] =\beta^2q^*(1-q^*)-2\beta^4(q^*)^2d_0\,,
    \end{align*}
    where $d_0 = 1-4q^* + 3b^*$.
    Substituting $h_0(M)=1-M^2$ yields
    \allowdisplaybreaks
    \begin{align*}
        A_{11,0}(0)
        &=\begin{pmatrix}
            2\beta^2q^*-2\beta^4q^*d_0&0\\
            0&\beta^2
          \end{pmatrix}\,,\\
        A_{12}(0)
        &=\frac12\begin{pmatrix}
            -1+\beta^2d_0&-2\beta^2\sqrt q^*\,(q^*-b^*)\\
            0&-1
          \end{pmatrix}\,,\\
        A_{22}(0)
        &=\frac14\begin{pmatrix}
            r&\sqrt q^*\,r\\
            \sqrt q^*\,r&1-q^*
          \end{pmatrix}\,.
    \end{align*}
    Only the first block depends explicitly on the Hessian choice, while the other blocks use the AMS scalar law.  
    Using $1-q^*=\Delta+rq^*$ gives
    \allowdisplaybreaks
    \begin{align*}
        A_{22}(0)^{-1}
        &=4\begin{pmatrix}
            r^{-1}+q/\Delta&-\sqrt q^*/\Delta\\
            -\sqrt q^*/\Delta&1/\Delta
          \end{pmatrix}\,.
    \end{align*}
    The $(2,2)$ and $(1,2)$ entries of the Schur complement are therefore
    \allowdisplaybreaks
    \begin{align*}
        S_0(0)_{22}&=\beta^2-\Delta^{-1}=-\frac{s}{\Delta}\,,\\
        S_0(0)_{12}&=\frac{\sqrt q^*\,s}{\Delta}\,.
    \end{align*}
    For the remaining scalar Schur complement, evaluate the quadratic form at $(1,\sqrt q)^{\top}$. Substituting the displayed inverse and using $d_0=\Delta-2rq^*$ and $s=1-\beta^2\Delta$ yields
    \allowdisplaybreaks
    \begin{align*}
        S_0(0)_{11}+\frac{sq^*}{\Delta} &=3\beta^2q^*-2\beta^4q^*d_0 -\frac{(s+2\beta^2rq^*)^2}{r} -q\beta^4\Delta =-q\beta^2s-\frac{s^2}{r}\,.
    \end{align*}
    Consequently, for $z=(\rho,a)^{\top}$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:hybproof-negative-squares}
        z^{\top}S_0(0)z =-\frac{s}{\Delta}(a-\sqrt q\,\rho)^2 -\left(q\lambda^2s+\frac{s^2}{\kappa}\right)\rho^2\,.
    \end{equation}
    Both coefficients are strictly positive, and the two squares vanish simultaneously only at $z=0$, so $S_0(0)\prec0$. At this point, a simple continuity argument implies that $S_0(\alpha_v) \prec 0$ for small enough $\alpha_v > 0$, and a choice of $\alpha_v \leqslant \min\{s/2,1/2\}$ yields a strict upper bound of $0$ on the objective function. \qedhere
<!-- end prf-8 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:uniform-local-strong-convexity}
    Let $\{m_t\}_{t \geqslant 0}$ evolve according to the HD process, and $S_A(c_{\mathrm{TAP}})$ be as in \prettyref{def:good-points}. Then, 
    \[
         \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_{A,B}\!\left\{\forall t\in[0,T],\quad B_\square(m_t,\rho\sqrt n)\subseteq\mathcal S_A(c_{\mathrm{TAP}})\right\}=1-o_n(1)\,.
    \]
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- prf-9: body_md -->
We first show there are small $\ell_2$-balls around the true means $\{m_t\}_{t\geqslant 0}$ in which $\widehat{Q}(m_t) \succeq c(\beta,t) I_n$ at every \emph{fixed} $t$. At $t>0$, this uses contiguity between the planted and original model for $\beta < 1$ \cite[\S 2]{el2022sampling} as well as \cite[Proposition 4.8]{el2022sampling} along with a union bound. At $t=0$, we prove this directly by conditioning on the event that $\ensuremath{\left\lVert A \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}} \leqslant 2 + \frac{(1-\beta)^2}{8\beta}$ and the radius of the ball $\varepsilon(\beta,0) \leqslant \frac{(1-\beta)^2}{24\beta^2}$. We then use \cite[Lemma 4.9]{el2022sampling} through Doob's inequality to control the fluctuations of $m_t$ in a small time-interval $(t-\delta_t,t+\delta_t) \subseteq [0,T]$ and finally construct a ``tube'' composing balls for any finite sub-cover of the interval $[0,T]$ conditioned on the fixed-time convexity estimates and the small-time stability estimates.
 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Fixed-time convexity balls.}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 For every fixed $t>0$, \cite[Proposition 4.8]{el2022sampling} immediately implies that, in the planted model,
\[
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_{A, B_t}\left\{\left\lVert{m_t - \widehat{m}^{k-1}(A,y_t)}\right\rVert_2 \leqslant \varepsilon'(\beta,t)\right\} \geqslant 1 - o_n(1)\,,
\]
for any $\varepsilon'(\beta,t) > 0$ and $\widehat{m}^{k-1}(A,y_t)$ denoting the $k$-th output of the AMP iteration of AMS initialized with tilt $y_t$. Invoking \prettyref{thm:hybrid-local-convexity} we obtain parameters $\varepsilon''(\beta,t)$ and $k'(\beta,t)$ so that 
\[
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_{A,g_0}\left\{\forall u \in B_\square(\overline{m}_{k'(\beta,t)-1}, \varepsilon''(\beta,t)\sqrt{n}),\,\nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot) \geqslant \varepsilon''(\beta,t)\right\} \geqslant 1 - o_n(1)\,.
\]
Choose $k(\beta,t) \geqslant \max\{k,k'(\beta,t)\}$ and set $\overline{m}_{k(\beta,t)-1} = \widehat{m}^{k-1}(A,y_t)$, along with a choice of radius $\varepsilon(\beta,t) = \frac{1}{2}\min\left\{\varepsilon'(\beta,t), \varepsilon''(\beta,t)\right\}$. Then, a union bound yields 
\[
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_{A,B_t}\left\{\forall u \in B_\square(\overline{m}_{k(\beta,t)-1}, \varepsilon(\beta,t)\sqrt{n}),\,\nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot) \succeq c(\beta,t)I_n\right\} \geqslant 1 - o_n(1) \,,
\]
where we invoke contiguity to transfer back to the original SK law from the planted model. \\
To obtain the same statement at $t=0$, use $m_0=0$ and choose $\delta_A = \frac{(1-\beta)^2}{8\beta}$ and $\varepsilon(\beta,0) = \frac{(1-\beta)^2}{24\beta^2}$ so that $\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_A\left\{\left\lVert{A}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant 2+ \frac{(1-\beta)^2}{8\beta}\right\}\geqslant 1-o_n(1)$. Conditioning on this event, for every $u\in B_\square(0,\varepsilon(\beta,0)\sqrt n)$, the bounds $D(u)\succeqI_n$ and $uu^{\top}/n\preceq \frac{\left\lVert{u}\right\rVert^2_2}{n}I_n$ yield
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot) &= \beta^2\left(1-\frac{\left\lVert{u}\right\rVert^2_2}{n}\right)I_n -\beta A + D(u) -\frac{2\beta^2}{n}uu^\mathsf{T} \succeq \left(1+\beta^2-\beta\left\lVert{A}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}} -\frac{3\beta^2}{n}\left\lVert{u}\right\rVert^2_2\right)I_n\\
    &\succeq \left((1-\beta)^2 - \frac{(1-\beta)^2}{8}-\frac{(1-\beta)^2}{8}\right)I_n \succeq\frac{(1-\beta)^2}{2}I_n\,.
\end{align*}
Set $c(\beta,0)=(1-\beta)^2/2$. Thus, for every fixed $t\in[0,T]$, there is a deterministic tuple $(c(\beta,t),\varepsilon(\beta,t))$ for which $\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_{A,B}\left\{\forall u \in B_\square(\overline{m}_{k(\beta,t)-1},\varepsilon(\beta,t)\sqrt{n}),\,\nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot) \succeq c(\beta,t)I_n\right\}\geqslant1-o_n(1)$, and we denote these events $\mathcal E_{n,t} := \left\{\forall u \in B_\square(\overline{m}_{k(\beta,t)-1},\varepsilon(\beta,t)\sqrt{n}),\,\nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot) \succeq c(\beta,t)I_n\right\}$.
 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Fluctuations across small time intervals.}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
  
For each $t\in[0,T]$, choose a deterministic $0<\delta_t\leqslant \frac{\left(\varepsilon(\beta,t)(1-\beta)\right)^2}{128}$ and set
\begin{align*}
    a_t&:=\max\{0,t-\delta_t\}\,,\qquad b_t:=\min\{T,t+\delta_t\}\,,
\end{align*}
and $J_t:=(t-\delta_t,t+\delta_t)\cap[0,T]\,$. Note that $J_t$ is an open neighborhood of $t$ relative to $[0,T]$. By the proof of \cite[Lemma 4.9]{el2022sampling} and \cite[(4.24)]{el2022sampling},
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    &\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_{A,B_t}\left\{\sup_{r\in[a_t,b_t]}
       \frac{\left\lVert{m_r-m_{a_t}}\right\rVert_2^2}{n} \geqslant q(b_t)-q(a_t) + \frac{\varepsilon(\beta,t)^2}{64}\right\} \leqslant o_n(1)\,,\\
    &q(b_t)-q(a_t)
    \leqslant \frac{(b_t-a_t)}{(1-\beta)^2}
     \leqslant\frac{\delta_t}{(1-\beta)^2}
     \leqslant\frac{\varepsilon(\beta,t)^2}{64}\,.
\end{align*}
In particular, the event $\mathcal F_{n,t}:=\left\{\sup_{r\in[a_t,b_t]}\frac{\left\lVert{m_r-m_{a_t}}\right\rVert_2^2}{n}\leqslant \frac{\varepsilon(\beta,t)^2}{32}\right\}$ has probability $1-o_n(1)$. On this event, for every $s\in J_t$,
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \left\lVert{m_s-m_t}\right\rVert_2
    &\leqslant\left\lVert{m_s-m_{a_t}}\right\rVert_2+\left\lVert{m_t-m_{a_t}}\right\rVert_2\\
    &\leqslant2\sqrt{nZ_{n,t}}
     \leqslant\frac{\varepsilon(\beta,t)}{2}\sqrt n\,.
\end{align*}
 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Finite sub-cover and a uniform bound.}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 The open sets $\{J_t:t\in[0,T]\}$ cover $[0,T]$ and compactness implies the existence of a finite subcover $J_{t_1},\ldots,J_{t_m}$. Denote the uniform radius and convexity parameters
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \varepsilon_M&:=\frac{1}{2}\min_{j \in [M]}\varepsilon(\beta,t_j)>0\,, \\
    c_M&:=\min_{j \in [M]}c(\beta,t_j)>0\,.
\end{align*}
Taking a union bound over the $M$ events $\bigcap_{j=1}^M\mathcal F_{n,t_j}\cap\mathcal E_{n,t_j}$ gives
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_{A,B}\left\{\mathcal \bigcap_{j=1}^M\mathcal F_{n,t_j}\cap\mathcal E_{n,t_j}\right\} \geqslant 1-o_n(1)\,.
\end{align*}
Note that each of the $j$ events concern the same disorder and SL path. On this event, fix any $s\in[0,T]$ and choose $j$ with
$s\in J_{t_j}$. For every $u\in B_\square(m_s,\varepsilon_M\sqrt n)$,
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \left\lVert{u-m_{t_j}}\right\rVert_2 &\leqslant\left\lVert{u-m_s}\right\rVert_2+\left\lVert{m_s-m_{t_j}}\right\rVert_2\\
    &\leqslant\left(\varepsilon_M+\frac{\varepsilon(\beta,t_j)}{2}\right)\sqrt n \leqslant \varepsilon(\beta,t_j)\sqrt n\,.
\end{align*}
This implies the containment $B_\square(m_s,\varepsilon_M\sqrt n) \subseteq B_\square(m_{t_j},\varepsilon(\beta,t_j)\sqrt n)$, and $\mathcal E_{n,t_j}$ gives $\nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot)\succeq c_MI_n$. Therefore,
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_{A,B}\!\left\{ \forall s\in[0,T],\ \forall u\in B_\square(m_s,\varepsilon_M\sqrt n), \ \nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot)\succeq c_MI_n\right\}&\geqslant 1-o_n(1)\,.
\end{align*}
If $T=0$, the same conclusion follows from the first part of the proof with $\varepsilon_M:=\varepsilon(\beta,0)/2$ and $c_M:=c(\beta,0)$.
 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{L\"owner order vis-a-vis $D(u)$.}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 Intersect the preceding event $\bigcap_{j=1}^M\mathcal F_{n,t_j}\cap\mathcal E_{n,t_j}$ with $\{\left\lVert{A}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant3\}$, which also has probability $1-o_n(1)$. Since $\left\lVert{u}\right\rVert^2_2/n \leqslant 1$ for any $u \in (-1,1)^n$, this gives the following L\"owner order lower bound
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot) &= \beta^2(1-\|u\|^2_2/n)I_n - \beta A + D(u) - \frac{2\beta^2}{n}uu^\mathsf{T} \succeq D(u)-\beta(2\beta + 3)I_n\,.
\end{align*}
Throughout the tube we also have $\nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot)\succeq c_MI_n$, so combining with the previous inequality gives
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    D(u) &\preceq \nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot) +\beta(2\beta + 3)I_n
     \preceq\left(1+\frac{\beta(2\beta + 3)}{c_M}\right)\nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot)\,.
\end{align*}
Choose
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    c_{\mathrm{TAP}}
    &=\frac{c_M}{c_M+\beta(2\beta + 3)}>0\,,
\end{align*}
and note the preceding bound immediately yields $\nabla^2\mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(u,\cdot)\succeq c_{\mathrm{TAP}}D(u)$ throughout the tube. Since $D(u)$ is PD, it follows that
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}_{A,B_t}\!\left\{\forall t\in[0,T],\, B_\square(m_t,\rho\sqrt n)\subseteq\mathcal S_A(c_{\mathrm{TAP}})\right\}
    &\geqslant 1-o_n(1)\,. \qedhere
\end{align*}
<!-- end prf-9 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:tap-convexity}
    Let $m \in \mathcal S_A(c_{{\mathrm{TAP}}})$ and condition on the ``master'' event $\mathcal E_A$. Then, there exist $0 < c_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(\beta,T), C_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(\beta) < \infty$, such that
    \[
        C_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(\beta)^{-1}D^{-1}(m) \preceq \widehat{Q}(m) \preceq c_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(\beta,T)^{-1}D^{-1}(m). 
    \]
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- prf-10: body_md -->
Conditioning again on $\ensuremath{\left\lVert A \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}} \leqslant $ 3, and using the fact that $D(m) \succ I_n$ for any $m \in (-1,1)^n$, yields
    \allowdisplaybreaks
    \begin{align*}
        \nabla^2 \mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(m,\cdot) &= \beta^2(1-\left\lVert{m}\right\rVert^2_2/n)I_n - \beta A + D(m) - \frac{2\beta^2}{n}mm^\mathsf{T} \\
        &\preceq D(m) + \beta\left(\ensuremath{\left\lVert A \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}} + \beta^2\right)I_n \\
        &\preceq(1+3\beta+\beta^2)D(m)\,.
    \end{align*}
    By \prettyref{thm:uniform-local-strong-convexity}, $\nabla^2 \mathcal F_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}(m,\cdot) \succeq c_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}D(m)$ for any $m \in \mathcal S_A(c_{{\mathrm{TAP}}})$, conditioned on the desirable event $\mathcal E_A$. Inverting the Hessian and combining the PD-ness with the preceding upper bound yields
    \[
        \frac{1}{1+3\beta + \beta^2}D^{-1}(m) \preceq \widehat{Q}(m) \preceq \frac{1}{c_{{\mathrm{TAP}}}}D^{-1}(m)\, ,
    \]
    for any $m \in \mathcal S_A(c_{{\mathrm{TAP}}})$. \qedhere
<!-- end prf-10 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:positive-part-cubic}
The matrix map $Z\mapsto Z_+^3$ is twice continuously Fr\'echet
differentiable on $\mathrm{Sym}_n(\mathbb R)$.
For every symmetric
$Z$ with $\ensuremath{\left\lVert Z \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}\leqslant1$, symmetric directions $H,K$, and
$p\in\{2,\infty\}$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:positive-part-cubic-derivatives}
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{aligned}
    \ensuremath{\left\lVert {d(Z_+^3)[H]} \right\rVert_{L^{p}}}
    &\leqslant 4\ensuremath{\left\lVert {H} \right\rVert_{L^{p}}},\\
    \ensuremath{\left\lVert {d^2(Z_+^3)[H,K]} \right\rVert_{L^{2}}}
    &\leqslant 10\ensuremath{\left\lVert {H} \right\rVert_{L^{2}}}\ensuremath{\left\lVert K \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
For $z\ne0$, the elementary arctangent integral gives
\[
    \int_0^\infty\frac{z^4}{z^2+t^2}\,dt
    =|z|^3\left[\arctan\!\left(\frac{t}{|z|}\right)\right]_0^\infty
    =\frac{\pi}{2}|z|^3.
\]
The same identity holds at $z=0$, since the integrand vanishes for $t>0$.
Thus $z_+^3=(z^3+|z|^3)/2$ and this generalizes to the matrix case (consider a basis where $Z$ is diagonal) as
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:positive-part-cubic-integral}
    Z_+^3
    =\frac{Z^3}{2}
     +\frac1\pi\int_0^\infty Z^4(Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1}\,dt.
\end{equation}
We apply a partial fraction decomposition. For $t>0$,
\[
    (Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1}
    =\frac{(Z-itI_n)^{-1}-(Z+itI_n)^{-1}}{2it},.
\]
Letting $S_{\pm} = (Z \pm itI_n)^{-1}$, the product and inverse rules and the
non-commutative H\"older inequality yield
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{alignat}{2}
\label{eq:quadratic-resolvent-derivative}
    \ensuremath{\left\lVert {d[(Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1}][H]} \right\rVert_{L^{p}}}
    &= \ensuremath{\left\lVert {\frac{-S_-HS_-+S_+HS_+}{2it}} \right\rVert_{L^{p}}}
    &&\leqslant t^{-3}\ensuremath{\left\lVert {H} \right\rVert_{L^{p}}},\\
\label{eq:quadratic-resolvent-derivative-2}
    \ensuremath{\left\lVert {d^2[(Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1}][H,K]} \right\rVert_{L^{2}}}
    &= \ensuremath{\left\lVert {\frac{S_-HS_-KS_-+S_-KS_-HS_- - \dots}{2it}} \right\rVert_{L^{2}}} &&\leqslant 2t^{-4}\ensuremath{\left\lVert {H} \right\rVert_{L^{2}}}\ensuremath{\left\lVert K \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}.
\end{alignat}
For $0<t\leqslant1$, the identity $(Z^2+t^2I_n)(Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1} = I_n$ gives a cancellation of the integrand of \prettyref{eq:positive-part-cubic-integral}:
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    Z^4(Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1}
    &=Z^2-t^2I_n+t^4(Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1},
    \\
    d[Z^4(Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1}][H]
    &=ZH + HZ+t^4\cdot d[(Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1}][H],
    \\
    d^2[Z^4(Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1}][H,K]
    &=KH + HK +t^4\cdot d^2[(Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1}][H,K].
\end{align*}
For $t\geqslant1$, instead differentiate the integrand directly with the product rule, using
$\ensuremath{\left\lVert (Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1} \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}\leqslant t^{-2}$.
Combining both cases therefore gives, when $\ensuremath{\left\lVert Z \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}\leqslant1$,
\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
    \ensuremath{\left\lVert {d[Z^4(Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1}][H]} \right\rVert_{L^{p}}}
    &\leqslant
    \begin{cases}
        3\ensuremath{\left\lVert {H} \right\rVert_{L^{p}}},&0<t\leqslant1,\\
        (4t^{-2}+t^{-3})\ensuremath{\left\lVert {H} \right\rVert_{L^{p}}},&t\geqslant1,
    \end{cases}\\
    \ensuremath{\left\lVert {d^2[Z^4(Z^2+t^2I_n)^{-1}][H,K]} \right\rVert_{L^{2}}}
    &\leqslant
    \begin{cases}
        4\ensuremath{\left\lVert {H} \right\rVert_{L^{2}}}\ensuremath{\left\lVert K \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}},&0<t\leqslant1,\\
        (12t^{-2}+8t^{-3}+2t^{-4})\ensuremath{\left\lVert {H} \right\rVert_{L^{2}}}\ensuremath{\left\lVert K \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}},&t\geqslant1.
    \end{cases}
\end{align*}

The integrand itself has operator norm at most $\min\{1,t^{-2}\}$.
The same estimates, with larger numerical constants, hold on $\ensuremath{\left\lVert Z \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}<2$.
