    By the resolvent identity, $DR = I - MR$. Hence $\Delta(DR) = - M_1(\Delta R) - (\Delta M)R_0 $. then apply \prettyref{lem:resolvent-difference-dR} and $\ensuremath{\left\lVert M \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}} \leqslant C_1$ and $C_2 = 1 + C_1\gamma^{-1}$.

     
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Part \ref{lem:resolvent-difference-dDRk}.}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 

    Use the discrete non-commutative product rule to compute 
    \[\Delta(DR^k) = (DR)_1\Delta(R^{k-1}) + \Delta(DR)R_0^{k-1}.\]
    Then apply \prettyref{lem:resolvent-difference-dDR} and \prettyref{lem:resolvent-difference-dRk} and, for the $\ensuremath{\left\lVert {\Delta W} \right\rVert_{L^{2}}}$ term, $C_1C_2^2\gamma^{-(k-1)} + (k-1)C_2^3\gamma^{-(k-2)} = (kC_2-1)C_2^2\gamma^{-(k-2)}$.

     
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{Part \ref{lem:resolvent-difference-dDRkD}.}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 
    Use the discrete non-commutative product rule to compute 
    \[\Delta(DR^kD) = (DR)_1\Delta(R^{k-1}D) + \Delta(DR)(R^{k-1}D)_0,\]
    then apply \prettyref{lem:resolvent-difference-dDR} and \prettyref{lem:resolvent-difference-dDRk} and collect terms.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- lemma-25: body_md -->
\label{lem:option-c-tap-free-endpoint}
Let $0<\beta<1$, $D\succeqI_n$, and $a=\beta^2\operatorname{tr}_n(D^{-1})$. Then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:option-c-tap-free-gap}
    aI_n-\beta S+D\succeq(1-\beta)^2I_n.
\end{equation}
For all
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:option-c-tap-cutoff}
    0<\gamma_Y\leqslant\frac{(1-\beta)^4}{4(1+2\beta^2)^2},
\end{equation}
it is true uniformly in $D\succeqI_n$ that
\begin{equation*}
    (I_n+D^{-1}(aI_n-\beta S))(I_n+(aI_n-\beta S)D^{-1})
       \succeq4\gamma_YI_n.
\end{equation*}
For every such $D$ and $a$,
\[
    E_{M_n}\!\left[D(aI_n-\beta S+D)^{-2}D\right]
       =\frac{I_n}{1-\beta^2\operatorname{tr}_n(D^{-2})}.
\]
<!-- end lemma-25 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
We first show that the range of the subordination function allows one to conclude a gap between $a=\beta^2\operatorname{tr}_n[D^{-1}]$ and the supremum of the bulk spectrum of the free convolution of $\beta S$ and $-D$ when $\beta < 1$. We then use the inverse identity and analyticity of the subordination function at $z=a$ to explicitly evaluate $F$ and its derivative to compute the free target.
 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{The free gap.}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 
 
The real inverse-subordination map for $\beta S-D$ \cite[Lemma B.18]{davies2026potential} is
\[
    u\longmapsto u+\beta^2\operatorname{tr}_n[(D+uI_n)^{-1}].
\]
For $u>\beta-1$, it is defined to the right of $\operatorname{Spec}(-D)$, and its derivative is strictly positive since
$\beta^2\operatorname{tr}_n[(D+uI_n)^{-2}]<1$.
The support characterization in
\cite[Proposition 2.2]{capitaine2011free} identifies this interval with part of the right exterior branch. Letting $u\downarrow\beta-1$ gives
\[
    \sup\operatorname{Spec}(\beta S-D)
       \leqslant\beta-1+\beta^2\operatorname{tr}_n[(D+(\beta-1)I_n)^{-1}].
\]
Indeed, the image is an interval connected to $+\infty$ and disjoint from the free support. Subtracting this upper bound from $a$ yields
\begin{align*}
    a-\sup\operatorname{Spec}(\beta S-D)
    &\geqslant(1-\beta)\left[
       1-\beta^2\operatorname{tr}_n\!\left(D^{-1}(D+(\beta-1)I_n)^{-1}\right)
       \right]\\
    &\geqslant(1-\beta)^2,
\end{align*}
because $D^{-1}(D+(\beta-1)I_n)^{-1}\preceq\beta^{-1}I_n$.
Faithfulness of the trace identifies the free support with the operator spectrum, proving \eqref{eq:option-c-tap-free-gap}.

 
  \par\addvspace{\smallskipamount} 
  \noindent\textit{The product cutoff and the target.}\hspace{0.5em}\ignorespaces 

The inverse identity and the free gap imply
\begin{align*}
    \ensuremath{\left\lVert (I_n+D^{-1}(aI_n-\beta S))^{-1} \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}
       &=\ensuremath{\left\lVert (aI_n-\beta S+D)^{-1}D \right\rVert_\mathsf{op}}\\
       &\leqslant1+\frac{a+2\beta}{(1-\beta)^2}
        \leqslant\frac{1+2\beta^2}{(1-\beta)^2}.
\end{align*}
Thus the free product is at least
$(1-\beta)^4(1+2\beta^2)^{-2}I_n$, and
\eqref{eq:option-c-tap-cutoff} implies the required buffer.

Let $F=F_{\beta,-D}$ be the subordination function. The inverse-subordination map above has positive derivative at $u=0$ and takes $0$ to $a$. This implies its (exterior) inverse satisfies
\[
    F(a)=0,\qquad
    F'(a)=\frac{1}{1-\beta^2\operatorname{tr}_n(D^{-2})}.
\]
Subordination under a non-commutative conditional expectation \cite[Theorem 3.1]{biane1998processes} gives $E_{M_n}[(zI_n-\beta S+D)^{-1}]=(F(z)I_n+D)^{-1}$. Note that $z=a$ is outside the free spectrum by \eqref{eq:option-c-tap-free-gap}, and so $\partial_z F(z)$ exists at $z=a$. Evaluating the derivative and conjugating by $D$ proves the asserted free target.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- corollary-5: body_md -->
\label{cor:overlap-mag-conc}
    For $0<\beta<1$ and any fixed time $0 \leqslant t \leqslant T(\beta) < \infty$, and every positive integer $k \in \mathbb Z_{+}$, 
    \[
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A,B_t}\left\langle(R_{12}-q^*)^{2k}\right\rangle_t \leqslant \frac{C(k)}{n^k} + C(\beta,k)e^{-c(k,t)n}\,,
    \]
    and
    \[
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A,B_t}\left\langle(M-m^*)^{2k}\right\rangle_t \leqslant \frac{C'(k)}{n^k} + C'(\beta,k)e^{-c(k,t)n}
    \]
<!-- end corollary-5 -->
<!-- prf-11: body_md -->
This follows straightforwardly after invoking \cite[Lemma 2.1]{li2026overlap}, which implies that there is a unique pair $(m^*,q^*) \in [0,1)^2$ that solve the fixed-point equations of \cite[Theorem 5.1]{davies2026potential} for $\beta < 1$. An immediate consequence of this, is that the proof of \cite[Theorem 5.2]{davies2026potential} implies magnetization concentration for $\beta < 1$ as well. For overlap concentration, note that the entire argument of \cite[Theorem 5.1]{davies2026potential} which verifies requirements (1) through (7) in the proof are valid provided that $q^*(\lambda) = q^*$ and $\beta^2 \leqslant \lambda$ even when $\beta < 1$. For the first, we replace the use of \cite[\S 5.6]{davies2026potential} with \cite[Lemma 2.1]{li2026overlap}, and for the latter, observe that $F(\lambda,q)$ \cite[\S 2.3]{LM19} continues to have a unique supremum over $q$ for $\lambda \leqslant 1$. \qedhere
<!-- end prf-11 -->
<!-- lemma-26: body_md -->
\label{lem:spectral-self-stability}
    Let $Y^*$ be a random variable that is almost-surely finite. Set $T:=\tanh(Y^*)\in[-1,1]$.  Define
    \[
        \mu_r:=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[T^r],\qquad r\in\{1,2,3,4\},
    \]
    and
    \[
        \mathbf M:=\begin{pmatrix}
            1-4\mu_2+3\mu_4 & 2(\mu_1-\mu_3)\\
            \mu_3-\mu_1 & 1-\mu_2
        \end{pmatrix}.
    \]
    Then for every $\beta < 1$,
    \[
        \rho(\beta^2\mathbf M)\;<\;1.
    \]
<!-- end lemma-26 -->
<!-- prf-12: body_md -->
The proof of \cite[Lemma 5.31]{davies2026potential} implies that, with $T = \tanh(Y^*) \in [-1,1]$,
\begin{align*}
    \mathbf{M} = \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{X}(T)]\,,
\end{align*}
where
\[
    \mathbf{X}(z) := (1-z^2)\begin{pmatrix}
        1-3z^2 & 2z \\
        -z & 1
    \end{pmatrix}\,.
\]
Set $\mathbf{S}:=\operatorname{\mathsf{diag}}(1,\sqrt{2})$ and define 
\begin{align*}
    \mathbf{K}(z)
    &:= \mathbf{S}\mathbf{X}(z)\mathbf{S}^{-1} = (1-z^2) \begin{pmatrix}
        1-3z^2 & \sqrt{2}\,z \\
        -\sqrt{2}\,z & 1
    \end{pmatrix}\,,
\end{align*}
which is similar to $\mathbf X(z)$. We show that $\|\mathbf{K}(z)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant 1$ for every $z\in[-1,1]$ by proving that the slack matrix $\mathbf R(z) := I - \mathbf K(z)^\mathsf{T} \mathbf K(z) \succeq 0$. To see this, let
$u:=z^2\in[0,1]$ and note that a direct computation gives
\begin{align*}
    \mathbf R (z)_{22} &= u^2(3-2u)\,, \\
    \det\left(\mathbf R (z)\right) &= u^4(u^2-6u+6)\,.
\end{align*}
For $0<u\leqslant 1$, both quantities are positive, since
$3-2u\geqslant 1$ and $u^2-6u+6\geqslant 1$. The Schur-complement criterion, therefore, gives $\mathbf{R}(z)\succeq 0$ (since $\mathbf{K}(0)=I_2$ and $\mathbf{R}(0)=0$). This yields 
\[
    \mathbf{K}(z)^{\top}\mathbf{K}(z)\preceq I_2\,.
\]
$\mathbf{S}$ is deterministic, and convexity of the operator norm gives
\begin{align*}
    \|\mathbf{S}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
    = \|\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{K}(T)]\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leqslant_{\text{Jensen's}} \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\|\mathbf{K}(T)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigr]
    \leqslant 1\,.
\end{align*}
Finally, similarity preserves the spectral radius, giving
\[
    \rho(\beta^2\mathbf{M}) = \beta^2\rho(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{S}^{-1}) \leqslant \beta^2 \|\mathbf{S}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leqslant \beta^2 <_{\beta < 1} 1\,. \qedhere
\]
<!-- end prf-12 -->
<!-- theorem-9: body_md -->
\label{thm:covar-estimate}
    Assume overlap and magnetization concentration as given in \prettyref{cor:overlap-mag-conc} and the spectral radius bound for the self-stability operator $\mathbf M$ defined in \prettyref{lem:spectral-self-stability}. Then, for $0 < \beta < 1$,
    \[
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{A,B_t}\left\lVert{\widehat{Q}^{-1}(m_t)Q(m_t) - I_n}\right\rVert_F^2 \leqslant \varepsilon(\beta,t)\, ,
    \]
    for every $t \in [0,T(\beta)]$ with $\sup_{t\in[0,T(\beta)]}\varepsilon(\beta, t) < \infty$.
<!-- end theorem-9 -->
<!-- prf-13: body_md -->
The proof follows by observing that, in the proof of \cite[Theorem 4.1]{davies2026potential}, all estimates in \cite[Lemmata 4.11--4.16]{davies2026potential} rely only on, after application of Stein's lemma and algebraic identities involving independent replicas, three things:
    \begin{enumerate}[itemsep=0.2em]
        \item Magnetization and overlap concentration (cf. \cite[Theorems 5.1--5.2]{davies2026potential}).
        \item Enhanced concentration for linear and cubic observables of bulk deviations (cf. \cite[\S 5.4]{davies2026potential}).
        \item Exact cavity estimates for the ``heavy'' cancellations (cf. \cite[\S 5.4]{davies2026potential}).
    \end{enumerate}
    The first point is directly valid for $0 < \beta < 1$ due to \prettyref{cor:overlap-mag-conc}. The second and third point rely on the fact that magnetization and overlaps have first moments which fluctuate on the $O(1/n)$ scale \cite[\S 5.4]{davies2026potential} and that various denominators in the second-order Taylor expansions used to compute exact cavity estimates via self-reducibility do not diverge \cite[\S 5.4]{davies2026potential}. A simple inspection of the argument in \cite[\S 5]{davies2026potential} with the strengthened spectral radius bound $\beta^2 \rho\mathbf M < 1$ and the fact that $\beta^2 \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{Y*}[\operatorname{sech}^{2k}(Y^*)] < 1$ for $0 < \beta < 1$ immediately implies the validity of all estimates in \cite[\S 5]{davies2026potential}. \qedhere
<!-- end prf-13 -->
<!-- lemma-27: body_md -->
\label{lem:nishimori-third} 
    Fix $t \geqslant 0$ and let $z \sim \mathcal N(0,1)$. Denote $Y^* = \beta^2m^* + t + \sqrt{\beta^2q^* + t}\,h$ where $(m^*,q^*)$ solve the fixed-point equations in \cite[Theorem 5.1]{davies2026potential}. Then, 
    \[
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_z\left[\tanh^3(Y^*)\right] = \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_z\left[\tanh^4(Y^*)\right]\,.
    \]
<!-- end lemma-27 -->
<!-- prf-14: body_md -->
By \cite[Lemma~2.1]{li2026overlap}, we have $m^*=q^*$ for $0<\beta<1$. Following the same notation and strategy as in \cite[Lemma 2.1]{li2026overlap} and \cite[\S 5.6]{davies2026potential}, let $r:=t+\beta^2q^*\geqslant0$ so that $Y^*\sim\mathcal{N}(r,r)$. If $r=0$, then $Y^*=0$ almost surely
    and both expectations vanish. Therefore, assume that $r>0$ for the rest of the proof.
    
    Let $p_r$ denote the density of $Y^*$. As in the proof of \cite[Lemma 2.1]{li2026overlap} a direct computation gives
    \begin{align*}
        p_r(-y)&=e^{-2y}p_r(y)\,.
    \end{align*}
    Since $\tanh(y)=(1-e^{-2y})/(1+e^{-2y})$, it follows that
    \begin{align*}
        p_r(y)-p_r(-y) &=\tanh(y)\bigl(p_r(y)+p_r(-y)\bigr)\,.
    \end{align*}
    Pairing the contributions from $y$ and $-y$, and using the oddness
    of $\tanh^3$ and the evenness of $\tanh^4$, yields
    \begin{align*}
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\tanh^3(Y^*)]
        &=\int_0^\infty \tanh^3(y)
          \bigl(p_r(y)-p_r(-y)\bigr)\,dy \\
        &=\int_0^\infty \tanh^4(y)
          \bigl(p_r(y)+p_r(-y)\bigr)\,dy \\
        &=\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}[\tanh^4(Y^*)]\,. \qedhere
    \end{align*}
<!-- end prf-14 -->
<!-- lemma-28: body_md -->
\label{l:ars}
Suppose that \prettyref{a:ars} is run with $\widetilde Q$, $\widetilde R$, and parameters $C_1, C_2$  satisfying the following.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \label{i:esm}
    (Sampling error) $\widetilde Q$ is a distribution such that $\operatorname{TV}(\widetilde Q, Q)\leqslant \varepsilon_{\textup{sample}}$.
    \item \label{i:ewt}
    (Tails of ratio)
    Let 
    \[
    C(\varepsilon) = \inf_C \left\{{C}:{\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_Q\left( {\frac{d P}{d Q} \wedge C} \right)\geqslant 1-\varepsilon}\right\}, 
    \]
    and suppose $C_1\geqslant C(1/2)\vee 1$, $C_2\geqslant C(\varepsilon_{\textup{weight}})$. 
    \item \label{i:ert}
    (Ratio error) 
    Let $G_1$, $G_2$ be events such that 
    \begin{align*}
    G_1 &\subseteq \left\{ {\frac{\widetilde R}{R}\in [0,e^{\varepsilon_{\textup{ratio}}}]} \right\}\\
G:&= G_1\cap G_2 \subseteq 
\left\{ { \frac{\widetilde R}{R} \in [e^{-\varepsilon_{\textup{ratio}}}, e^{\varepsilon_{\textup{ratio}}}] } \right\}
    \end{align*}
    and $\widetilde Q(G_1^c)\leqslant \delta_1$, $P(G_2^c)\leqslant \delta_2$. (Note these probabilities can involve randomness in the algorithm.)
\end{enumerate}
Then letting $\widehat P$ be the output distribution, with probability $\geqslant 1-\delta'$ over the $N$ initial samples,
\[
\operatorname{TV}(\widehat P, P) \leqslant \varepsilon_{\textup{weight}} + \delta_2 + 384C_1C_2(\delta_1 + \varepsilon_{\textup{ratio}} + \varepsilon_{\textup{sample}})  
\]
and if 
$\varepsilon_{\textup{weight}} + \delta_2 + 96C_1C_2(\delta_1 + \varepsilon_{\textup{ratio}} + \varepsilon_{\textup{sample}})\leqslant \frac{1}{2}$, the acceptance probability is at least $\frac{1}{192C_1C_2}$.
<!-- end lemma-28 -->
<!-- prf-15: body_md -->
Define the good sets on path space (1) the ratio between the ideal and approximate distribution is bounded, (2) the ratio between the scaffolding and approximate distribution is not too small, and (3) the weights are bounded,
    \begin{align*}
        G_1 &= \left\{ {\frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^s}{d \widehat{\mathbf{P}}_T}(\gamma_T)\leqslant L} \right\}&
        G_2 &= \left\{ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T}\geqslant c_3} \right\}&
        G_3 &= \left\{ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T}\leqslant C_1} \right\}
    \end{align*}
    and let their complements be $B_1,B_2,B_3$, respectively. 
    Now
    \begin{multline}
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T} \frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2} \mathds{1}_{S_T\cap (B_1\cup B_2 \cup B_3)}} \right]\\
        \leqslant 
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T}\frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2}\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap B_1} } \right]
        + \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T}\frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2}\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap G_1\cap B_2} } \right]
        + \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T}\frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2}\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap G_1\cap G_2\cap B_3} } \right].
        \label{e:3-bad}
    \end{multline}
    We bound each term separately. 
    Note that if $\mu\ll \nu$, then 
    \begin{align}
    \nonumber
        \operatorname{KL}(\mu\|\nu)& = \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_\mu \left( {\log \frac{d \mu}{d \nu}} \right)\mathds{1}_{\frac{d \mu}{d \nu}\geqslant 1} + \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_\mu\left( {\log \frac{d \mu}{d \nu}} \right) \mathds{1}_{\frac{d \mu}{d \nu}< 1} 
        \geqslant  \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_\mu \left( {\log \frac{d \mu}{d \nu}} \right)\mathds{1}_{\frac{d \mu}{d \nu}\geqslant 1} - 1
        \geqslant \mu\left( {\frac{d \mu}{d \nu}\geqslant L} \right)(\log L)-1\\
        \implies
        \mu\left( {\frac{d \mu}{d \nu}\geqslant L} \right) & \leqslant \frac{\operatorname{KL}(\mu\|\nu) + 1}{\log L}.
        \label{e:kl-markov}
    \end{align}
    To apply to our setting, we define the stopped process by
    \begin{align*}
        dx_t^{\mathrm{stop}} &= \mathds{1}_{t<\tau'} f_t\,dt + dB_t 
    \end{align*}
    and similarly define $\widehat{x}_t^{\mathrm{stop}}$. Let their laws up to time $T$ be $\mathbf{P}_t^{\mathrm{stop}}$ and $\widehat \mathbf{P}_t^{\mathrm{stop}}$, respectively. 
    Then
    \begin{align}
    \nonumber
        \operatorname{KL}(\mathbf{P}_T^{\mathrm{stop}} \|\widehat \mathbf{P}_T^{\mathrm{stop}} )
        & = \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{P}_T^{\mathrm{stop}}} \log \frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^{\mathrm{stop}}}{d \widehat \mathbf{P}_T^{\mathrm{stop}}}\\
    \nonumber
        &= -\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}  
    \int_0^{T\wedge \tau'} \langle\widehat f_t - f_t, dB_t\rangle + \frac{1}{2} \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}
    \int_0^{T\wedge \tau'} \left\Vert {\widehat f_t - f_t}\right\Vert^2dt\\
    &= \frac{1}{2} 
    \int_0^{T\wedge \tau'} \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left\Vert {\widehat f_t - f_t}\right\Vert^2\,dt \leqslant \frac{1}{2} \int_0^T\varepsilon(t)^2\,dt.
    \label{e:KL-stop}
    \end{align}
    by assumption 1 and Girsanov's theorem.
    
    For the first term of \eqref{e:3-bad}, noting that $x_{[0,T]}= x_{[0,T]}^{\mathrm{stop}}$ if $T<\tau'$ so that $\mathbf{P}_T^s = \mathbf{P}_T^{\mathrm{stop}}$ restricted to $\{T<\tau'\}$,
    \begin{align*}
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T}\frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2}\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap B_1} } \right]
        &= \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}} \left[ {\frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^s}{d \widehat{\mathbf{P}}_T}(\widehat{x}_{[0,T]})\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap B_1}(\widehat{x}_{[0,T]})} \right]= \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap B_1}(x_{[0,T]})} \right]\\
        &\leqslant \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left[ {\frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^s}{d \widehat\mathbf{P}_T}(x_{[0,T]})>L} \right]
        \leqslant \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left[ {\frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^{\mathrm{stop}}}{d \widehat\mathbf{P}_T^{\mathrm{stop}}}(x_{[0,T]}^{\mathrm{stop}})>L} \right] + \mathbf{P}_T[\tau'\leqslant T]\\
        &\leqslant_{\eqref{e:kl-markov}\text{, assumption 1}} \frac{\operatorname{KL}( \mathbf{P}_T^{\mathrm{stop}}\|\widehat\mathbf{P}_T^{\mathrm{stop}})+1}{\log L} + \delta'
        \leqslant_{\eqref{e:KL-stop}} \frac{\frac{1}{2} \int_0^T \varepsilon(t)^2dt + 1}{\log L} + \delta'\leqslant  
        \frac{\varepsilon}{3} + \delta'
    \end{align*}
    by choosing $L=\exp\left( {\frac{3}{\varepsilon}\left( {\frac{1}{2} \int_0^T \varepsilon(t)^2dt+1} \right)} \right)$. 
    Next, 
    note that directly by assumption 3, $\mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}[S_T\cap B_2]\leqslant \varepsilon_3$.
    Hence we can bound the second term
    \begin{align*}
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T}\frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2}\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap G_1\cap B_2} } \right]
        &= \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^s}{d \widehat \mathbf{P}_T}(\widehat{x}_{[0,T]})\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap G_1\cap B_2}} \right] \leqslant L \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}[S_T\cap B_2] \leqslant L\varepsilon_3.
    \end{align*}
    Finally, note that under $S_T\cap G_1\cap G_2$, 
    $\frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2}= \frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^s}{d \widehat\mathbf{P}_T}\big/ \frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T} \leqslant \frac{L}{c_3}$, so 
    for the third term, by assumption 2,
    \begin{align*}
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T}\frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2}\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap G_1\cap G_2\cap B_3} } \right]
        &\leqslant 
        \frac{L}{c_3}\mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T}\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap B_3}} \right]
        \leqslant \frac{L\varepsilon_1}{c_3}.
    \end{align*}
    Putting everything together, 
    \begin{align*}
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T} \frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2}\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap (B_1\cup B_2 \cup B_3)}} \right]
        &\leqslant \frac{\varepsilon}{3} + \delta'+ L\varepsilon_3 + \frac{L\varepsilon_1}{c_3}=\varepsilon+\delta'
    \end{align*}
    by choosing $\varepsilon_3 = \frac{\varepsilon}{3L}$ and $\varepsilon_1 = \frac{c_3\varepsilon}{3L}$. Hence 
    \begin{align*}
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{S_T}
            \frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T} \frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2}\wedge \frac{C_1L}{c_3}
        } \right] &\geqslant \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T} \frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2}\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap G_1\cap G_2 \cap G_3}} \right]\\
        &= \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{S_T}\frac{d \mathbf{P}_T^s}{d \widehat  \mathbf{P}_T}(\widehat{x}_T)} \right] - 
        \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T} \frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2}\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap (B_1\cup B_2\cup B_3)}} \right]\\
        &= \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\mathds{1}_{S_T}(x_{[0,T]})} \right] - \mathop{{}\mathbb{E}}\left[ {\frac{e^{\widehat{w}_T}}{\widehat Z_T} \frac{R_2(\widehat{x}_T)}{Z_2}\mathds{1}_{S_T\cap (B_1\cup B_2\cup B_3)}} \right]
        \geqslant 1-\delta-\delta' - \varepsilon.
    \end{align*}  
    The first part now follows from \prettyref{l:ars} applied to path measures $Q = \widehat \mathbf{P}_T$ and $P=\mathbf{P}_T$, with $\varepsilon_{\textup{weight}} = \varepsilon+\delta+\delta'$, $\delta_1 = \delta_2 = \varepsilon_{\textup{ratio}} = \varepsilon_{\textup{sample}} =0$, and then taking the marginal at time $T$. 
    Note we allow probability $\varepsilon$ of failure (in the quantile estimate or rejection sampling taking too many trials).

    For the second part, let $\widetilde Q = \widetilde  \mathbf{P}_{T}$ be the path measure of $(\widetilde x_t)_{t\in [0,T]}$. 
    Let 
    $\widetilde R = e^{\widetilde w_T}\widetilde R_2$, and $R = e^{\widehat{w}_T}R_2$. 
    Note that assumption \ref{i:je-path-error} gives assumption \ref{i:esm} of \prettyref{l:ars} with $\varepsilon_{\textup{sample}}=\varepsilon_4$. Consider the events
    \begin{align*}
        G_1' &= \left\{ {\frac{\widetilde R_2}{R_2}\in [0,e^{\varepsilon_4/2}]} \right\} \cap 
        \left\{ {|\widetilde{w}_T - \widehat{w}_T|\leqslant \frac{\varepsilon_4}{2}} \right\}
        \subseteq \left\{ {\frac{\widetilde R}{R}\in [0,e^{\varepsilon_4}]} \right\}.
        \\
        G_2' &= \left\{ {\frac{\widetilde R_2}{R_2}\in [e^{-\varepsilon_4/2}, e^{\varepsilon_4/2}]} \right\},
    \end{align*}
    and note $G_1'\cap G_2'\subseteq \left\{ {\frac{\widetilde R}{R}\in [e^{-\varepsilon_4},e^{\varepsilon_4}]} \right\}$. It remains to check assumption \ref{i:ert} of \prettyref{l:ars}. We check
    \begin{align*}
        \widetilde \mathbf{P}_T({G_1'}^c)\leqslant \widetilde \mathbf{P}_T^s({G_1'}^c) + \widehat\delta   
        &\leqslant \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}}\left( {\frac{\widetilde R_2(\widetilde{x}_T)}{R_2(\widetilde x_T)}\nin [0,e^{\varepsilon_4/2}]} \right)
        + \widetilde\mathbf{P}_T^s\left( {|\widetilde{w}_T - \widehat{w}_T|>\frac{\varepsilon_4}{2}} \right) + \widehat \delta
        \leqslant_{\eqref{i:je-ewt}, \eqref{i:je-ert}(a)} \frac{\varepsilon_4}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_4}{2} + \widehat\delta = \varepsilon_4 + \widehat\delta\\
        \mathbf{P}_T({G_1'}^c)
        &= \mathop{{}\mathbb{P}} \left( {\frac{\widetilde R_2(\widetilde{x}_T)}{R_2(\widetilde x_T)}\nin [e^{-\varepsilon_4/2},e^{\varepsilon_4/2}]
        } \right) \leqslant_{\eqref{i:je-ert}(b)} \varepsilon.
    \end{align*}
    Thus the assumption holds with $\varepsilon_{\textup{weight}} = \varepsilon+\delta+\delta'$, $\delta_2=\varepsilon$,  $\delta_1=\varepsilon_{\textup{sample}}=\varepsilon_4+\widehat\delta$, and  $\varepsilon_{\textup{ratio}}=\varepsilon_4$. The final TV error of the sampled distribution $\widehat P$ is
    \begin{align*}
\operatorname{TV}(\widehat P, P)
&\leqslant \varepsilon_{\textup{weight}} + \delta_2 + 384 C_1'C_2'(\delta_1+\varepsilon_{\textup{ratio}}+ \varepsilon_{\textup{sample}})\\
&\leqslant \varepsilon +\delta+\delta'+\varepsilon + 384 {C_1'}^2(\varepsilon_4+ \widehat \delta+\varepsilon_4+\varepsilon_4+\widehat\delta) = 5\varepsilon + \delta +\delta'+  768{C_1'}^2\widehat \delta.
    \end{align*}
    Allowing probability $\varepsilon$ of failure then gives the result.
<!-- end prf-15 -->
